WRITE_CLICKS keeps five members (Apply to ZONA, Put back, Store on ZONA, Clear, Target); `Clear` is excused from the register's re-casing rule as `Put back` is, because settledBody's 'changing page on your ZONA clears it' (D-23) is the wire's verb and not the control. THE BOX is DeviceSlot's: 44px on both axes, 16px inline padding, the boundary token as the border, the workspace ground, two 14px fixed line boxes - the label (13px 600) over an aria-hidden caption (12px, the quiet ink - DeviceSlot's caption register, not the plan's 11px, which section 12 reserves for the tracked uppercase micro role) that reads the closed record's reason when disabled and NOTHING when live, so the shape is fixed and the accessible name is the label alone; the sr-only span the button points at reads clearLine(page) when live and the reason when not; aria-busy with clearingLabel(page) through the one leg; disabled with a real attribute for the three reasons, through a write and through a page target not at rest (13-12), never hidden (DEGR-02); the hover moves the boundary to the action colour; no radius, no transition, no animation. THE ROOM RULE, because the plan's caption did not fit the plan's box: the three reasons run 172.6 / 205.6 / 254.2 wide at 12px (chromium and webkit agree), so the box is 206.6 / 239.6 / 288.2 with one inside; the header's row - mark 268, nav 373, two 48px gaps, 20px padding each side - leaves the zone 503 at 1280, 247 at 1024 and exactly the reserved 218 at 768, where the mark and the nav are already at their minimum. So the connection zone is now a flex-grow inline-size container on the reserved 218 basis (Header.svelte: gap 12px; flex 1 1 var(--connection-w); container-type inline-size) and Clear.svelte renders its caption under @container (min-width: 480px) - 480 being the widest pair the store can put together (no-snapshot 288.2 + 12 + `ZONA connected` 160.3 = 460.5; incapable 239.6 + 12 + `Not in this browser` 161.1 = 412.7; no-session 206.6 + 12 + `Disconnected · draft retained` 220.1 = 438.7) rounded up to the next ten; below it the box carries the label alone (68 wide) and the reason lives in the description. Measured on the served build after the change: the header is 76 tall at 1920 / 1280 / 1024 / 768 on /, /playground/arc/ and /sandbox/?new in both engines, as before; 209 (app) / 113 (intro) at 375, as before; document scrollWidth never above clientWidth; at 1280 the Clear box (288 chromium, 240 webkit) sits at x 813 / 847 with the slot at 1113 / 1099; at 1024 the 68px box at 777 / 763 beside the slot at 857 / 843; at 375 on the app pages the pair sits at the end of its own 335px row. Previous status, retained (13.1-03): THE HANDLES DRAG, EVERY ELEMENT HAS ITS OWN COLOUR, AND THE SANDBOX READS AS PAGE 3. 13.1-03, the fourth plan of Phase 13.1's serial order, answered bench line 3 (13.1-CONTEXT D-03: 'you should be able to resize each element by draggin its points. it has visible points but they cannot be resized by that as of now. also, each element should have its own color and better visualization, now its too minimal'). THE DRAG: pointerdown on one of the plate's eight handles - named nw n ne w e sw s se, each with a 16px hit square beneath it under data-testid surface-handle-hit and never surface-handle (SANDBOX_HANDLE_HIT in layout.ts: 8 is the page's drawn size, 44 would swallow the cells around a corner, 16 keeps the cell clickable) - stops the cell click and captures the pointer on the plate; every move computes the box the handle would make from the cell under the pointer (a corner two edges, an edge one, the anchor the opposite side, never below 1 x 1, never off the surface) and draws it as the proposed bounds 13-16 already drew; release hands the box ONCE to onresize, which the route wires to the editor's new resizeSelectedTo(box): the same applyEdit as Width and Height under the resize kind, keyed and sealed on the spot so one drag is one Undo, the same box no edit and no entry (applyEdit builds a fresh array for every accepted patch, so applyPatch's identity guard could never tell - the box is compared in the method), a refusal returning geometry's own line - section 16's overlap line naming the other region, the off-surface line, the too-small lines - with the surface the same object, Play and an empty selection silent. The refusal shows in the plate's status line (role=status, announced) until the next pointer or key on the plate or until the surface moves under it - held in $state.raw, because a $state proxy of the surface never equalled the editor's own reference and the line silently vanished on the served page; the e2e title found it. NO DRAG IS EVER REQUIRED (13-16's rule) is restated in the plate's header and is true: the numeric fields and the keyboard route are untouched, and sandbox-ui.spec.ts test 2's no-drag clause is inverted honestly - exactly one method starting resize, none matching pointer or hover, the pointermove handler never calling onclick or onresize. Test 7 (+1) drives the commit, the overlap, the off-surface, the too-small, the same-box, Play and no-selection cases in node with no pointer; e2e/sandbox.e2e.ts's new title (+1 / +1, chromium) places a fader and a button by clicks, pulls the fader's south-east handle three steps to the cell one right and two down of its far corner and reads 3 x 8 off the fields with the proposed bounds visible under the pointer, pulls its east handle onto the button and reads the overlap line with the width unchanged and no entry, presses and releases the north handle in its own cell for no entry, and takes the drag back with one Undo - at a 900-tall viewport, because the app frame's footer overlays the plate's bottom row at 720 (deferred-items A.1: a pointerDOWN must land on a visible handle; the moves after it are captured) - red against the pre-plan build on a fresh server. Previous status, retained (13.1-04): EDIT COLOR OPENS DOWN, SEAMLESSLY, AND THE PICKER INSIDE DID NOT MOVE A LINE. 13.1-04, the second plan of Phase 13.1's serial order, answered bench line 7's first half ('Edit color should not be pop up window in the left upper corne but instead open down seamlessly to edit color.', 13.1-CONTEXT D-08): Swatch.svelte's <dialog> opened with showModal() - 13-09's popover, the platform's trap and top layer, the backdrop click and the focus return written there - is deleted; each swatch row's Edit color is a toggle (aria-expanded on its own knob, aria-controls to the block's id) reading Close (F.8's word, kept; POPOVER_CLOSE not renamed, three files read it) while its block is open, and the block is a role=group labelled by the row's own label, rendered in the row's flow under the value line only while open on that knob, the rows below moving down, nothing floating, no backdrop; ColourPicker inside it keyed on the knob and handed selectedId once; a click on another row's toggle moves the block. FOCUS SIMPLIFIED: no trap, no return - the toggle never leaves the DOM; Escape inside the block (a delegated keydown on the group, BrowseGrid's svelte-ignore precedent, stopping propagation so KeepConfirm's window listener does not also fire) closes it and puts focus on the row's toggle after tick(), the file's only .focus(). The picker renders only while open, so 13-09's 'rendered once, closed' reason is retired with the dialog and the e2e that reads the rails opens the block first. Previous status, retained (12-05): THREE BENCH NOTES ANSWERED FROM SOURCE, AND THE ONE NUMBER TWO DOCUMENTS DISAGREED ABOUT TURNED OUT TO BE TWO NUMBERS. ARC's fix is eighteen characters and the diagnosis is the whole of it: the stop tap sets s.s=0, and the rate branch on the next line re-arms the swirl for any MOVE whose column differs - and a resting finger sends a MOVE every sample (probe A Q1, on the user's own module). The Timer advances no phase while s.s is 0, so the CC held and the pad turned. The preview could never have shown it, because a click delivers a DOWN and an UP and no MOVE at all, which is why 11-09.1's stop/resume test is green and this one was needed. The library was considered and refused by measurement rather than by preference: 12-07's hysteresis suppresses a MOVE inside one CELL, and cell 40 spans x 57..71, which is r 14 to 18 - five of the thirty-one rate steps - so an in-cell wobble IS a real rate change. CONSOLE'S REVERSAL IS THE SECOND READING OF ONE SENTENCE AND BOTH READINGS ARE ON THE RECORD: the entry header now writes down all three shapes it has shipped, and lua-smoke test 8 was rewritten rather than deleted, with its picture assertion read off the sim because the wire is now silent for the very move the stage is about. It fits at 852 with 56 free, which is the number 12-09 must fit Q into. NINE PADS IS THE PLAN'S LESSON ABOUT MEASUREMENT: the plan forbade picking between 550 and 556 and asked for a reason the other figures were wrong, and the answer is that NEITHER IS WRONG - they measure the shipped card and a tuned one, and the six characters between them are the marker withChange rewrites when it deletes preset. The shape character was measured rather than argued, twice: through its inputs (n=6, 4,127 option values either side) and through a stamp minted at a fixed index vector, byte-identical. Five files the plan's file list does not name had to move for the default to land at all, and one of them - presets.spec.ts's PLAN_ID regex - asks in its own failure message to be widened deliberately, in the plan that needs it. THE WIDGET RULE IS KIND-BLIND AND IT READS ITS VALUES, and the reason is a shipped knob: STARFIELD's edge is `feel` with "soft" and "hard", so an n-only rule would have rendered it as a word row labelled "1 of 2" - strictly worse than the rail. AND THE DEFAULT MOVE EXPOSED A FINDING NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR: the reachability sweep's two-pass split rests on a separability licence that is false on this card, because at 3x3 the compiler emits the chosen colour and a dimmed variant of it and at 4x4 it emits it once - so Pass B, which ranks colours at every other knob's default, now picks a literal that is not the dearest at the other grid. The true worst is still 640 of 908 and unmoved, colour-picker.spec.ts pins it independently and is green, the sweep's equality pins what the sweep can produce with all four measured corners beside it, and the item is in the phase's deferred-items.md with what a fix would cost. Nothing was hardware-verified: all three notes came from the user's bench and only their bench confirms these fixes.
Previous status, retained (12-04): THE VOCABULARY WAS KEPT HONEST A SECOND TIME AND FOUR SENTENCES NOBODY GATES WERE FOUND ALREADY UNTRUE. Seven FOR terms summing to 26 and six FEELS summing to 52, every one of the thirteen carried by two or more, zero singletons, both rules passing on the data UNWEAKENED. Three negative checks, three reds, every term named: FOR keys at 1 (arriving without a plant, because it is the state of the tree at the task 01 commit), FEELS still at 5, and the value-is-a-facet-member walk naming harmonic and the value keys no facet declares - the last two planted as single asserted replacements and restored from scratch copies compared by sha256 either side. AND CHECK A DID NOT FIRE WHERE THE PLAN SAYS: the zero-singleton assertion is unreachable as a first red, because the FOR floor of two and the FEELS floor of six both run before it and a singleton violates a floor by definition - it restates rather than guards. THE PLAN'S TASK 01 CANNOT BE GREEN and its own task 02 depends on that, which is why the task 01 commit message says the tree is red at it and why. EIGHTEEN FILES WERE TOUCHED THAT files_modified DOES NOT NAME, and the sharpest is a hard red: stamp-roundtrip.sweep.spec.ts asserts the colour-knob count (33 -> 27) and has been in NO plan's blast-radius table for four consecutive waves - it reconciles at 58 guarded + 27 exempt = 85 knobs, which independently confirms the recounted census. Three further literals no table named: stamp.spec.ts:482 (moved-format 16 -> 13), facets.spec.ts:69 (a THIRD fourteen, three lines under the second) and touch-guard.spec.ts:516's toContain('forge'). Four stale prose figures corrected with their drift NAMED rather than swapped: precise had been 7 since 11-15 while the header claimed 6; FOR_TERMS' declared descending order had been wrong by one pair since 11-14; lua-entries.sweep.spec.ts's colour-knob census was two waves out of date and is recounted, not subtracted, to 27 colour knobs across 15 of 17 entries and 85 knobs in all; and nineteen files said the closed vocabulary is sixteen or fourteen. sort.spec.ts's entries floor of 27 had ALSO been wrong for two waves and was green the whole time, because a floor only goes red when the listing falls THROUGH it - which is what a removal wave is for. docs/HARDWARE-AUDITION.md renumbered 1..22 with every cross-reference followed (MIRROR 11->10, CONSOLE 14->13, STAGE 17->16, POMODORO 23->20) and its five-row short list down to three. FRONT_DOOR asserted UNMOVED at 8; only EXCLUDED_FROM_ROW shrank, 21 -> 18. Nothing under src/vendor/ moved, ROADMAP.md is byte-unchanged, requirements mark-complete was skipped (five of the six named ids are already [x]; CAT-01 gains a dated Phase 12 qualifier instead and CAT-04 stays [ ] for the sixth time), docs/TESTING.md is left for 12-12 and named, and no agent touched a device or deployed anything.
Previous status, retained (11-14): THE CHECKPOINT WAS ANSWERED `new-entry` AND EXACTLY THAT WAS BUILT: THE RADAR PRESET STAYS AT RING POSITION 5 UNTOUCHED, AND RADAR POINTS CARRIES THE RADAR ASK AS A SECOND HAND-AUTHORED CARD BESIDE IT AND BESIDE SONAR. Task 01's measurement stands as the handover recorded it: the front door's `padsim` rule is LIVE for a reason its own comment does not give - Coverflow.svelte builds an engine for every ring entry, so a lua row puts 271 KB of Lua VM on the front page's first paint, which e2e/tuning.e2e.ts forbids in words - so the new card is registered in EXCLUDED_FROM_ROW with THAT reason rather than the curated-row boilerplate, the ring is untouched at eight, ported.ts is untouched at nine and radar.ts is untouched. SONAR IS REUSED CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER AND NAMED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE IN THE HEADER - the arming callback with its per-contact cell guard, the layer split with the always-lit centre on layer 0, the pending-list release, the 252/250/42 decay pair and the five-knob rack at SONAR's value counts - WITH THE ONE DIFFERENCE THE TWO NAMES IMPLY: SONAR sweeps an angle, a radar rolls rings out from the centre, so a cell's step is its Chebyshev distance and, as the consequence that forces rather than a second distinction, its direction is its pitch - eight compass directions walking a named scale and wrapping an octave. The bucket edges are centred with a half-bucket offset because, measured over all 81 cells before a line was authored, the raw floor put two ring-1 cells in one direction and six cells in another. EIGHT STEPS TO A PING AND FIVE RINGS ON THE PAD: for three steps the ring is past the edge and the Timer compares k against a distance that is 0..4 by construction - k is never an index - and that silence is what makes the wave read as a ring under a 42-tick trail rather than as a pad that glows permanently. COSTED AT THE RGB444 PICKER CORNER BEFORE AUTHORING: Setup 579 / Timer 281, 329 and 627 free, fixed points at every corner. THE FIRST SKETCH WAS WITHDRAWN ON A GATE THE PLAN DOES NOT NAME: an eight-entry scale table indexed straight by direction measured 560 / 281 and ran GREEN in every spec the plan lists, and the full quick suite then reddened view.spec.ts and knobs.lua.spec.ts, because the tune panel names every `scale` value from SCALE_WORDS - semitone SETS with mode names, derived from the catalog - and an invented set is red rather than a rail. The walk-and-wrap replaced it at nineteen characters and put SONAR's own values back in the rack. ONE TEST, lua-smoke 24 to 25: three points on three rings in three directions, and over EXACTLY sixteen steps ring 2's point sounds one step after ring 1's and ring 4's three steps after, ring 1 sounds every eight steps, all eight ring-1 cells are lit on the wave layer at the fire and no ring-4 cell is, every note is released one step later, and then the middle point taken away is SILENT while the other two keep sounding - the clause the plan names as the one a happy-path test misses - and put back, returns. Two faults in the test's own framing were found on its first two runs: a seventeen-step window held three fires of an eight-step ping, and a note started in the window's last step was reported as hung. EIGHT PLANTS, every one restored from a scratch copy with sha256 compared either side, all red on the clause they were aimed at - and THREE OF THEM RE-PLANTED FIRST: one came back green because it never wired its sticky table into the Timer, and two reddened on Lua parse errors rather than on their clause. A NEW ENTRY IS A NEW ROW IN EVERY CENSUS, the fourth time: filter.spec.ts, colour-picker.spec.ts and stamp-roundtrip.sweep.spec.ts re-counted with their reasons, and a fourth surface the two-word name found - audition.spec.ts's name parser claimed `RADAR POINTS` as `RADAR`, which no live entry is called, and was widened by name rather than the name bent, claiming the same names for every row already there. Counts, as carried name plus delta from 11-15: PREV_FILES 84 +0, PREV_TESTS 868 +1 = 869, PREV_E2E 86 titles +0 by grep (the suite not run; 105 runs carried), BASE_CHECK 581 +1 = 582, sweep members 4 19 +0, catalog 28 +1 = 29 with the split 9 + 20, static/og 28 +1 = 29 at 172,699 bytes of which the other 28 are 11-15's 166,516 to the byte, audition 24 +1 = 25. Lua-route Pass A +2,000 (5 x 5 x 5 x 16), Pass B +4,096, lua-entries +60; compiler Pass A 20,782 unmoved for the ninth time. D-11-10-b MOVED INCIDENTALLY: the plan quoted SONAR at 432 / 279 from the audition table and sonar.ts's own header has read 558 / 279 since 11-08, so the tally is five rows examined, TWO stale, thirteen unchecked, and the row is left for 11-16 with the rest of the table because this wave did not re-author SONAR. `requirements mark-complete` NOT RUN: the plan's list carries CAT-04, deliberately unchecked four times over in writing, and CONT-01, which this plan did not touch. Nothing is hardware-verified: the note-off is proved against a log and not a note, and audition row 25 is the only thing that closes it.
Previous status, retained (11-15): THE ONE ENTRY THIS PHASE ADDS RATHER THAN REPAIRS, AND THE NATURAL WAY TO WRITE IT MISSED THE BUDGET BY TWO HUNDRED AND TEN. The user asked, after the bench notes, for "a pitch and modwheel configs, leftside a pitchwheel rightside a modwheel on the ZONA beautifully visualized on the module". WHEELS is one card carrying both: pitch on columns 0-3, mod on columns 5-8, and COLUMN 4 A LIT DIVIDER THAT BELONGS TO NEITHER AND ANSWERS TO NO FINGER - nine columns do not halve, and sharing the middle one would make the boundary a lie. THE ASYMMETRY IS THE ENTRY, so the two halves differ in four places rather than in colour: pitch is fourteen-bit BEND on status 224 and springs home on a lift, mod is a seven-bit CONTROLLER and stays where it was left; pitch is one lit row that travels, mod is a bar that fills from the bottom. THE ONE ASSERTION THE CARD EXISTS FOR: after a bend and a lift, THE LAST BEND THE TIMER EMITS IS EXACTLY 8192 - the integer, not "near centre" - proved at all four spring speeds, and proved TOGETHER WITH THE LIGHT by comparing the whole 243-byte frame byte-for-byte against the frame Setup painted. A plant that lands the wire two flat WHILE THE PICTURE COMES HOME reddens on that clause alone, naming both values, which is exactly the failure the request named: a held note still bent after the finger has gone. THE BUDGET WAS COSTED BEFORE THE ENTRY WAS AUTHORED and the natural architecture - one paint function per wheel, colour and phase written on every repaint - measured 1118 of 908 at the RGB444 picker corner, TWO HUNDRED AND TEN OVER, with the Timer sitting at 191 and 717 free that it could not use, because touch_cb has to repaint IMMEDIATELY and a Timer re-armed on every sample never fires at all (shuttle.ts's trap). It fits at 895 with THIRTEEN FREE - the tightest margin in the catalog - because ONE function paints all eighty-one cells with no Setup paint loop of its own, and because self.r and self.k get no sentinel values at all: nil is the sentinel, and that is nineteen characters of nothing. FOUR SHAPES WERE PRICED OUT RATHER THAN ARGUED AWAY: a deflection ribbon 939, a centre detent 922, a two-layer divider 917, a mod bar with a marker on its edge 914. FOUR LEVERS THE CARD DOES TAKE WERE PRICED BY REMOVAL: the second layer 54, the ten-bit unlock 53, the origin lock 50, the held-finger guard 22. THE TEN-BIT UNLOCK NEEDED A THIRD REASON THAT 11-13'S RULE DOES NOT COVER: the pitch wheel owns the WHOLE y axis, so axis ownership says it gains nothing - but its OUTPUT IS WIDER THAN THE AXIS, and a locked axis would offer 128 raw positions to a fourteen-bit value, giving a seven-bit controller wearing a fourteen-bit label. Measured on the wire: 512 distinct bends over a half-travel drive, reaching 16383, on a pad that draws nine rows. THE PLAN'S CENTRAL PREMISE IS FALSE AND THE TREE HAS SHIPPED THE COUNTER-EXAMPLE SINCE 09-07: "a stored Timer makes the entry animated whatever its picture is doing" is true only of an entry whose SETUP arms the Timer and whose body re-arms UNCONDITIONALLY. WHEELS does neither - the spring is armed by a lift and stops by not re-arming, never by gtt(index,0) - so frames.json reports animating FALSE at all five sampled ticks and the declared motion is `static`, exactly as FORGE's 373-character Timer has for six phases. THE PLAN ALSO ASKS FOR THE OPPOSITE ONE PARAGRAPH LATER ("stop the Timer when the spring lands"), and this wave took the honest half. THE PLAN'S gmbs CHECK CANNOT PASS AS WRITTEN: it asks for `grep -n "gmbs"` over the entry file to be EMPTY and for the header to explain why gmbs is wrong, and the grep returns four hits, all prose - and a card that DID call gmbs would satisfy it just as badly. Replaced with two assertions no comment can move: no HID out-call in the RENDERED Lua at any of twenty-four knob renderings, and the host's HID log EMPTY after the whole drive. A plant that adds one gmbs beside a correct bend reddens both, the second with 1,752 recorded mouse sends. SIX NEGATIVE CHECKS, every one restored from a scratch copy with sha256 compared either side, and TWO OF THEM FOUND FAULTS IN THE CHECKS RATHER THAN IN THE CARD: a plant that walks the bend to 8190 makes the spring oscillate for ever and HUNG the suite instead of failing it, because the settle loop was unbounded - a hang is not a red, and the loop is now bounded and asserts it terminated; and a plant that removed the gms call entirely reddened a precondition rather than the HID clause it was aimed at, so a second plant that merely ADDS a click was written to isolate it. THIS PLAN RAN AHEAD OF 11-14, WHICH IS OPEN AT A BLOCKING CHECKPOINT AND HAS SHIPPED NOTHING, and it was safe because the dependency is only the catalog count chain and that chain COMMUTES: this plan's term is +1 in every branch. NO ABSOLUTE TOTAL IS ASSERTED ANYWHERE - every count is a carried name plus a delta, 11-14's branch table was NOT applied because there is no recorded answer to apply, and 11-14 can still land in any of its four options. Counts: PREV_FILES 84 +0 (wheels.ts is a SOURCE file, not a spec), PREV_TESTS 866 +2, PREV_E2E 86 titles / 105 runs +0 with the suite RUN, BASE_CHECK 580 +1, sweep members 4 19 +0, catalog 27 +1 = 28 today plus T14 whenever 11-14 lands, preset/Lua 9 + 19, static/og 28 files, audition 24 rows. Three RECORDED censuses in three files no plan names were re-counted rather than silenced - filter.spec.ts, colour-picker.spec.ts and stamp-roundtrip.sweep.spec.ts - which is the third occurrence of that shape and now a pattern. D-11-15-a filed: host-surface.spec.ts cannot express an entry-installed `self:` method, though self.touch_cb proves the construct is legal and universal; `self.D` ran green in a live host and was WITHDRAWN rather than widening a safety gate inside a wave that authors a card the user will flash, and snake.ts's Setup-local-plus-Timer-copy duplication ships instead, closed by the byte-for-byte frame assertion. Nothing is hardware-verified: the spring is proved against a LOG and not against a note, and audition row 24 is the only thing that closes it.
Previous status, retained (11-13): THE ASK WAS FOR TWO CONTROLS AND THE PLAN READ THREE, SO THE THIRD WAS PRICED RATHER THAN ARGUED AWAY. The bench note was "STRIP: this is just an XY pad, it should be two faders, one crossfader at the bottom and the big one, sending midi independently" - a COUNT followed by an APPOSITION NAMING THE TWO THINGS COUNTED, where "one X and the big one" contrasts the first member against the second. The three-control reading has to discard the sentence's own "two faders" and leaves "the big one" with no referent, and the plan's must-haves, interfaces and success criteria all specify three on @CC, @CC+1 and @CC+2. TWO SHIPS, and the rejected reading was written out as Lua and MEASURED: 1,088 characters of 908 at the RGB444 picker corner, ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY OVER, in an entry with no Timer - it can only be made to fit by moving the repaint into a Timer, and any entry with a stored Timer classifies as `animated`, which the plan's own objective says a card of static faders should not be. So a future wave that wants three controls starts from a number rather than from an argument. THE PLAN'S BUDGET QUOTES THE DEFAULTS CORNER: "638 of 908, 270 free" is the knobs at their defaults, not even the all-longest declared corner of 646 the entry's own header quoted, and at the picker corner the Setup was 662 with 246 free - STRIP is the ELEVENTH entry checked and the SEVENTH found quoting a corner that is not the picker one, and it is the first whose plan quoted the defaults. THE LAYOUT: the big fader owns rows 0..7 across all nine columns, the crossfader owns row 8, and EVERY GESTURE IS LOCKED AT ITS ONSET TO THE CONTROL IT STARTED ON - s.o[i] written when e==4 or e>8 or s.o[i]==nil and read on every later sample - which is what makes "independently" a structural property rather than two controller numbers. Costed at 61 characters against a bare row test. THE ORIGIN LOCK BUYS ITS OWN BUG AND A PROBE FOUND IT: locking a contact to the fader means the fader branch now sees the coordinates the unlocked layout would have handed to the crossfader, and (910-y)*127//910 floors NEGATIVE there - measured through the real Lua host, a drag from y=800 to y=1023 put 15,14,...,1,0,-1,-2,...,-16 on controller @CC, sixteen negative controller values, no error raised, and EVERY GATE IN THE TREE GREEN. glim(...,0,127) is eleven characters and is why the shipped Setup is 875 at the picker corner with 33 free rather than 864. THE TEN-BIT UNLOCK SURVIVES AND THE PLAN'S REASON FOR KEEPING IT IS WRONG WHILE ITS CONCLUSION IS RIGHT: the test is not continuity, it is WHAT FRACTION OF AN AXIS A CONTROL OWNS. Firmware reports a locked axis as 0..127 by dividing the native 0..1023 by eight, so a control that owns a WHOLE axis and sends seven bits is already at full resolution - the crossfader is exactly that case and the shipped code says so by sending x//8, character for character what a locked axis would have reported - while the FADER owns eight rows of nine, would see only 114 of the 128 raw positions on a locked axis, and would lose fourteen codes at every position. Cost 44 characters, and HANGAR keeps its only worked example of txma/tyma, which is still STRIP's: the six other mentions in forge.ts, lattice.ts and lumen.ts are all prose saying it is the wrong answer for a cell-grid card. BOTH AXES ARE UNLOCKED THOUGH ONLY y NEEDS IT, and that is D-11-13-a: lua-host.ts:713-719 is a single axisMax handler behind both names writing ONE _coordMax, and enqueue clamps both coordinates against it, so an entry that wrote self:tyma(1023) alone would be driven in the browser with a ten-bit x it does not have on the module - every x*9//1024 in it would map the whole pad into the leftmost column on real hardware while the preview looked perfect, with nothing red anywhere. Fifteen characters buys agreement between the simulator and the firmware. EVERY DIVISOR IS DERIVED FROM WHAT ITS CONTROL OWNS, which is 11-07's CONSOLE lesson applied twice: 910 is BOTH the boundary and the divisor for the fader and the test asserts they are the same number, while the crossfader has NO divisor at all because it owns the whole width - the contrast is what makes the rule a rule. INDEPENDENCE IS MEASURED IN FOUR DRIVES: the crossfader across its row is 128 messages on controller 2 and none on 1 with the fader's bar still at the height Setup gave it; a contact that BEGINS on the crossfader and is dragged the entire height of the fader is 88 messages on 2 only with the bar still unmoved; the fader down its own rows is 128 messages on 1 and none on 2; and the overshoot - a contact that begins on the fader and runs off the bottom across the crossfader row while sweeping x by 520 units - is 16 messages on 1 only with THE MARKER NOT MOVING A CELL. Every drive moves its coordinate on every step, because the host's own change gate silently drops a repeated (event,x,y) per contact and a probe built out of identical samples would be defeated by the host rather than answered by the entry. Both controls reach 0 and 127 with ALL 128 CODES DISTINCT, and the fourteen-bit loss is recorded as an assertion rather than a comment - seven bits is asserted BEFORE full scale, because a control emitting 0..255 would fail "reaches 127" too and a check that reddens on the wrong clause leaves its own clause untested. THE FIRST LEGIBILITY ASSERTION IN THE REPOSITORY landed here and it is narrow: stated as SHAPE and naming no colour, because both hues are knobs a visitor may set to the same value - each fader row uniform across nine columns, exactly two states, the lit rows one contiguous run ending at the bottom, and the crossfader's row exactly one bright cell among eight VISIBLE ones. It proves the two controls draw DIFFERENT pictures, not that either is readable, so D-11-12-b stands and is amended rather than closed. THREE NEGATIVE CHECKS AND ONE OF THEM CAUGHT ITSELF: recombining the axes reddens the independence test naming both controllers ([1,2]); shifting the crossfader to x//4 reddens the SEVEN-BIT clause by name; and cutting the fader's numerator from 127 to 120 came back GREEN because String.replace took the FIRST occurrence, which is IN THE HEADER COMMENT - a comment participated in a measurement, and re-planted against the SETUP STRING it reddens on the full-scale clause naming the observed maximum 120. The test itself had to be relaxed before that plant could reach its own clause: the fader regex pinned the numerator at 127 and would have reddened on the shape of the source instead of on the wire. frames.json DID move, 163 lit bytes to 171 at all five ticks with a new hash, and animating is FALSE at all five so motion stays static, restsBlack stays false and the quiet line stays required - it moved too. STRIP HAS NO DEMO PATH AND CANNOT HAVE ONE, DEMO_PATHS holding ghost and morph only, so its social preview is its resting picture: inspected by eye after the build, a half-open cyan fader over a dim navy rail above an amber row with one bright centre cell - two controls, unmistakably two pictures. Description 89 to 106 of 110, the word "vernier" gone from the copy and from the labels. Tags unchanged with the histogram read BEFORE the question: precise SURVIVES seven bits because both controls resolve every one of the 128 codes of a 7-bit controller, which no other entry does, so the floor collision the plan feared does not arise and precise and still stay at 6. Five knobs, four values each, every id unchanged: shape character n before and after and the captured xn33333 still decodes restored, with `vernier` kept as a deliberately stale name because wild-stamps.json pins it. The audition's STRIP budget row moves 638 to 857 and, like SHUTTLE's, WAS ACCURATE before this wave - four rows checked in four waves, one stale, fourteen unchecked - and ROW 15 IS REWRITTEN because its whole subject was the mode-1 two-message expansion this card no longer makes. Nothing here is hardware-verified: independence proved against a simulated touch stream is a claim about a simulated touch stream, a real finger has width, and whether a stranger can say which control is which is row 15(c) and only a bench closes it.
Previous status, retained (11-12): A DOCUMENTED DECISION REVERSED WITH BOTH OF ITS REASONS ANSWERED, AND A DESIGN THAT DID NOT FIT UNTIL IT MOVED EVENTS. The bench note was "SHUTTLE: don't understand how it works, rework", and D-04 read that as a verdict on the behaviour. shuttle.ts:79-89 recorded two reasons for refusing to let a speed survive the lift, and a rewrite that discarded them silently is how a project relearns what it already knew. REASON 1 - a latch stoppable only by touching the exact centre column - is REVERSED and answered structurally: the stop is now the whole bottom row, nine cells wide, painted red the entire time there is anything to stop and dark otherwise, which is GHOST's lit-corner idiom from 11-11 reused rather than reinvented. The objection was never to latching; it was to a latch whose exit has no affordance. REASON 2 - a DOWNUP tap arrives with no lift behind it, so a tap that set a speed could never be cleared by the gesture that set it - is SOLVED BY REMOVING THE DEPENDENCY: this latch's exit is a PRESS, and code 9 delivers a press as completely as codes 4 and 1 do, so a coalesced tap on the red row is a full stop in one message. The consequence is that THERE IS NO TAP-VERSUS-HOLD RULE IN THE FILE and no branch anywhere reads contact duration - which also sidesteps the trap 11-09.1 named, since src/lib/sim/touch.ts never emits code 9 and any design turning on spotting one is invisible in the preview. THE THIRD REASON IS THE ONE THE BENCH ACTUALLY FILED, and it is a verdict on the behaviour rather than the copy: the indicator and the gesture SHARED NO GEOMETRY. The control was a horizontal axis across nine columns and the picture was a ring at radius two - a ring says turn me, nothing in it marked zero, and forward and reverse were the SAME COLOUR distinguished only by which way round the arc had grown. The bar is now rows 3 to 5, the centre column is a breathing white zero mark measured never to fall below 79 of 255, and the track carries each side's colour at one fifth brightness so the resting card answers which side is forward with no finger on it. THREE SHAPES WERE COSTED AS LUA BEFORE A LINE WAS AUTHORED and the plan's own budget quoted the wrong corner: 663 of 908 with 245 free is the all-longest DECLARED corner, and at the RGB444 picker corner the Setup was 683 with 225 free - SHUTTLE is the tenth entry checked and the SIXTH found quoting the wrong one. The lift-stops shape came in at 686 and fits; THE LATCHING SHAPE CAME IN AT 935, TWENTY-SEVEN OVER 908, while its Timer sat at 181 with 727 free; the hybrid that latches above a speed threshold fits at 601 and was REJECTED ON LEGIBILITY rather than price, because a rule that changes behaviour at an invisible threshold re-earns the bench note it was meant to answer. A request that cannot fit is a finding, not a failure - so the shortfall was reported to the architecture instead of to the design and the repaint moved out of touch_cb into the Timer, cell for cell unchanged: Setup 540 at EVERY corner (it now carries no colour token at all) with 368 free, Timer 542 at the picker corner with 366 free. PARITY_ALLOWANCES IS NOW EMPTY: its one row excused SHUTTLE for sending NOTHING on a fast tap, and the rewrite makes the fast and slow runs identical at 51 messages each, which test 5 requires the row to be deleted for. Both 11-02 gates are green with no new rows - DECLARED_EXCEPTIONS still 2, KNOWN_VIOLATIONS still 0 - and the onset guard is now EXAMINED rather than merely correct, because the live filter and the stop test are separate chains and (e==4 or e>8) has no e==1 in it to make the scanner skip it. TWO OF THE THREE NEGATIVE CHECKS FOUND GAPS RATHER THAN PASSES. Removing the math.max floor and driving the Timer period to zero froze the picture and stopped the keystrokes while the pad went on showing a speed it was not sending - and 105 server tests and six sweep tests stayed GREEN, frames.spec.ts included, because the resting picture is identical whether the Timer repaints it or not (D-11-12-a; nine of the eighteen hand-authored entries store a Timer). Giving forward and reverse the SAME colour - a pad whose direction cannot be read at all - reddened only frames.spec.ts, on a PIXEL HASH, and regenerating frames.json as any author would turned the whole tree green again: 23 files, 133 tests, plus six sweep tests (D-11-12-b). That is 11-11's demo-path finding generalised - every gate in the catalog reads well-formedness, budget, arithmetic and change, and none reads meaning. frames.json DID move this time and it had to, to 9 lit bytes at tick 0 and 57 afterwards, because Setup now paints only the mark and closes with gtt(0,20) so the Timer draws the card 20 ms after boot; animating is true at all five ticks so motion stays animated and restsBlack stays false, both re-derived from the fixture rather than carried. SHUTTLE HAS NO DEMO PATH AND CANNOT HAVE ONE - DEMO_PATHS holds ghost and morph only and listing.spec.ts fails any key that does not rest black - so its social preview can only ever show its resting picture, which now carries both direction colours and the zero mark but can never show the red stop row. The description moved to 104 of 110 characters and is written for someone who has never edited video: scrub, jog and transport are all gone. Tags unchanged, histogram checked BEFORE the question with precise and still still at the floor of 6. Six knobs, four values each, every id unchanged: shape character 2 before and after and the captured x2333333 still decodes restored, with arc and rest kept as deliberately stale names because wild-stamps.json pins them. The audition's SHUTTLE row moves to 540 / 538 and, unlike GHOST's, WAS ACCURATE before this wave - three rows checked in three waves, one stale, fifteen unchecked. Row 18 gains part (b): pick the module up cold and say which side is forward, where zero is and what happens when you lift, before anyone tells you. Nothing here is hardware-verified and whether the redesign ANSWERED the note is not claimed, because negative check 3 proves an unreadable SHUTTLE is fully green.
Previous status, retained (11-11): A BLANK PAGE, COSTED THREE WAYS BEFORE A LINE WAS AUTHORED, AND A RESET THAT WAS RELIGHTING THE CELLS IT CLEARED. The bench note was "GHOST: doesn't work reliably, the LED colors the pad and resetting is not reliable, need to redesign this from scratch", and the mechanism behind the third clause is now MEASURED rather than guessed: glp(a,l,p) writes the phase and touches NEITHER the rate NOR the timeout, and grid_led_tick does pha += fre on every tick a timeout is still running, so a cell mid-decay at rate 250 set to phase 0 is at PHASE 250 on the very next tick. Planted on the new entry's own shape with only the clear idiom swapped - one variable, everything else identical - the old form leaves three cells lit at every one of the 120 ticks after the reset and NEVER CLEARS THEM. THE OLD ERASE WAS CREATING THE CLASS-A FREEZE RATHER THAN UNDOING IT, and it only ever touched layer 1, so the ghost's own trail was outside it entirely. The clear is now glpfs(a,1,0,0,0) and glpfs(a,2,0,0,0) over all 81 cells - phase 0 AND RATE 0, the house idiom decay-idiom.spec.ts already skips by name because a layer that does not walk has no landing to check. THREE RESET GESTURES WERE SKETCHED AS LUA AND MEASURED BEFORE THE ENTRY EXISTED, at the RGB444 picker corner: a HELD press 533 Setup / 571 Timer, ONE lit corner cell 478 / 422, a TWO-CELL corner 542 / 457 - all three fit, so this was a choice and not a constraint, and the cheapest was NOT chosen for being cheapest. A hold has no affordance, so it answers "resetting is not reliable" with "and now you must also hold it for the right length of time"; two cells double the area a drag can accidentally start on and buy nothing. SHIPPED: screen cell 80 coloured 255,0,0 at Setup and lit by the Timer only while there is something to erase, so THE KEY EXISTS EXACTLY WHEN IT IS LIT - no dead square, and the marker IS the state indicator. It is red because the erase key is a function and not decoration and must stay legible at every setting of both colour knobs, and IT PULSES on a 280 ms cadence, which is load-bearing and was PROVED so: planting the steady marker the design rejected (rate 0) reddens the residue probe naming cell 80 layer 1 at phase 252 against an untouched run holding 0 - a check the plan never asked for, run because this phase's first standing warning says to suspect a green negative result, and it also proves the probe reaches GHOST rather than passing it vacuously. TWO MORE STRUCTURAL CHANGES: every onset now starts a FRESH recording, so a second drag REPLACES the first instead of being glued onto its end (the old recorder appended, and two drags made one incoherent loop); and s.h=e<9 closes the code-9 latch 11-02 handed forward BY NAME in one assignment - a contact is live if and only if it is not a press-and-lift in one message - so a fast tap records its point and goes straight to playback instead of arming a latch only a lift could clear. Fast tap 406 messages against a slow press's 406, identical, where the research measured 0 against 8. Previous status, retained (11-10): A CAPABILITY ADDED TO THE HOST ON A TEMPLATE ALREADY IN THE FILE, AND THE ONE ENTRY THAT NEEDED IT. The bench note was "LUMEN: should send HEX in sysex", and until this wave the sysex half could not be PREVIEWED at all: gmss was not among HANGAR's fifteen host globals, so an entry that sent one would have run on a real ZONA and raised "attempt to call a nil value" in its own catalog card - measured, by planting exactly that and reading the message. HOST_GLOBALS is now SIXTEEN. The recorder was chosen rather than copied: a THIRD log, HostSysex = { bytes }, beside HostMidi and HostHid, because a sysex message is a variable-length run of bytes - four of HostMidi's five fixed fields would be permanently zero and HostHid's call union is named for mouse clicks - and with NO call discriminant at all, because ZONA_REFERENCE.md:1249-1250 states that gms and gmss are the only MIDI emitters in the whole Grid global table, so the union would have exactly one member. recordSysex keeps the WHOLE payload in call order, framing included (gmss requires the CONFIGURATION to supply 0xF0 and 0xF7), and applies F2Ieq and nothing else - no seven-bit narrowing, deliberately, so an illegal byte stays visible instead of being hidden by a clamp. Every literal fifteen in src/ and docs/ was found by grep rather than remembered: 34 hits, exactly ONE of them a count of the host surface, moved, with the other 33 classified as correctly left. LUMEN now emits 240, 125, 70, 70, 53, 65, 48, 48, 247 - "FF5A00" for the anchor colour - with 0x7D, the non-commercial manufacturer id, in the byte a receiver reads as the sender's identity. Both encodings were costed before either was written: raw three-byte RGB 660 at the picker corner, seven-bit-split raw 693, ASCII hex 746 - and THE SHORT ONE IS NOT A CANDIDATE, because sysex data bytes are SEVEN-BIT and row 0 of this pad is the anchor colour exactly, so the first cell a visitor touches asks for 255. The 86 characters the raw form saves buy a message that is malformed at the top of the pad and fine at the bottom. The SETUP event carries the send, gated on "n~=s.c or e>3" - one message per colour, plus one on every fresh contact - and the Timer stays entirely unspent at 0 of 908, because a Timer that sends on every tick would emit the cursor's colour a hundred times a second whether or not anything changed. x*127//128 was measured and FIXED on both axes: it mapped 0..127 onto 0..126 and could never emit 127, and since this entry never calls txma or tyma the scale had nothing to scale - MINUS eighteen characters, so the fix paid for part of the ask. LUMEN's ladder at the RGB444 picker corner: 608 as 11-09.2 left it, 590 with the scales gone, 746 shipped, 162 free of 908. The depth half is exactly 11-09.2's verdict, carried forward by name and re-opened by nothing here - the knob delivers, row 0 cannot move, {1,2,3,4} is the whole legal travel - and its recorded non-delivery (the simulator says the bottom row walks 196,69,0 -> 27,9,0 and the bench says nothing moves) is carried to 11-16 rather than dropped. No new knob and no change to any knob's value count: 4 knobs, [4,4,3,4], total 15, shape character b, stamp wb00fff0 - byte-identical before and after, proved rather than asserted, so no LUMEN link is demoted from restored to older. frames.json byte-untouched and proved with a run that has no UPDATE_FRAMES. Five negative checks in all, every plant restored from a scratch copy with sha256 compared either side and no git checkout, restore, stash or clean at any point - and check 4 had to be RE-PLANTED TWICE, because the first two plants went red on the length clause and on the decode clause while the seven-bit clause it existed for never ran. That is this phase's standing warning in its inverse form: a check that reddens on the wrong clause leaves its own clause untested. AND THE FINDING THE MEASUREMENT PRODUCED: NOTHING IN HANGAR VALIDATES SEVEN-BIT SYSEX DATA. A planted raw-RGB send recorded 240, 125, 255, 90, 0, 247 with the 255 stored as-is and no assertion anywhere objecting; the vendored trap scanner has never heard of gmss (absent from _pad.ts's OUT_CALLS); so the only guard in existence is one clause of LUMEN's own test, covering LUMEN only. Recorded as D-11-10-a. Tests observed at 862, +3 - and THE PLAN DECLARED +2 while asking for three: task 01 wants two and task 02 wants a third, and the plan's own +2 term names only task 01's pair. Reported, not reconciled; check-counts 84 861 exits 1 and 84 862 exits 0, and both were run. Nothing is hardware-verified: a sysex message recorded into a list is not a sysex message a desk received.
Previous status, retained (11-09.1): TWO CHECKPOINT ANSWERS THAT WERE FEATURE WORK, ONE ONSET IDIOM, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT RETIRES A WHOLE CLASS OF GESTURE DESIGN. ARC now stops when you tap cell 40 and resumes on the next tap, and THE SIMULATOR WAS NEVER WRONG ABOUT IT: the entry had no stop, no resume and no toggle of any kind - s.r = 1 + x*31//127 is at least 1 at every x - so the open worry that the preview was lying about a shipped card CLOSES rather than being carried, on the user's own "i meant to add stopping and resuming tap as a feature". THE GESTURE WAS COSTED THREE WAYS AND THE HOLE IS NAMED IN REAL VALUES: cell 40 alone costs rate 14..18 at depth 56..70 from a press STARTING there (the 3x3 heart would have cost 11..21 at 42..84), and the hole is halved by the onset gating because a drag THROUGH the centre still sets both - asserted, at swirl rate 15 with 50 distinct values. A second contact is rejected for the second consecutive wave on the reason 11-09 found: i > 0 IS deliverable and a mouse still has one pointer. THE STOPPED PICTURE IS THE FROZEN SWIRL, NOT THE FROZEN HEART, because the heart alone reaches phase 0 - measured across 60 stop moments it lands anywhere in 0..254 and BELOW 16 ON 2 OF 60 - while the frozen swirl's brightest cell is 253 of 255, so a stopped ARC is still and plainly lit and never dark. Stopping is a separate flag multiplied into the phase step (s.h + s.r*s.s) and NOT a zeroed s.r, which the next touch would revive; self.f carries the swirl rate so the resume is exact rather than 4 -> 2; and the keeper re-arm gained "or s.s<1" because a stopped card has NO PHASE WRAP and 655 seconds after a stop layer 2 would have expired and taken the only visible sign of the stop with it. A STOPPED ARC GOES ON SENDING, 50 MESSAGES A SECOND, DELIBERATELY: MORPH's shouting was four DIFFERENT CCs racing for a MIDI-learn binding and ARC has ONE, MIDI is stateful so a host connecting mid-stop learns the held value, and a stop that went silent would be INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A TIMER THAT RAISED. MORPH's corner tap now emits exactly ONE message and it is that corner's, 4 -> 1 at the point a finger actually lands, and THE MEASUREMENT CAME FIRST AND DID NOT SHRINK THE TASK: the plan predicted 11-08's suppression might already deliver one from rest, and that is true ONLY on the single extreme pixel (0,0) of each block - at the CENTRE of a corner block the weights are 100/12/12/1 and all four leave, from rest and from elsewhere alike. Every probe is pinned to a point where all four weights are non-zero, so a one-message result cannot be the old behaviour wearing the new one's clothes. BOTH ENTRIES TAKE ONE IDIOM - the onset edge e==4 or e>8, cited to stage.ts and pad-sim.ts:228-241 - and NEITHER EARNED A DECLARED_EXCEPTIONS ROW, still 2. THE LARGEST FINDING IS THAT src/lib/sim/touch.ts NEVER EMITS EVENT CODE 9 AT ALL: a 300 ms press and the fastest press a pointer can make BOTH deliver 4 then 5, so "only a coalesced DOWNUP counts as a tap" would have been invisible in the browser - the same PREV-01 failure that has now killed three gesture options across two plans. SIX NEGATIVE CHECKS, FIVE RED AND ONE GREEN, all restored from scratch copies with sha256 compared either side: the plan's own load-bearing claim that "e==4 or e>=5" double-fires is FALSE AT THIS SITE because 11-02's ended guard already returns for e>=5 and e<9, so code 5 never reaches the toggle - and where the trap IS live (the toggle moved above the guard) it double-fires on the SLOW tap, not the fast one, so the plan names the wrong gesture. Stopping by s.r = 0 reddens the resume half at "1 distinct value over 50 messages"; removing the stopped picture reddens at "the swirl's rate is 4"; emitting all four reddens naming all four; emitting corner 1 regardless reddens at "Corner 2 owns cc 17; cc 16 arrived" WHILE THE COUNT CLAUSE STAYS GREEN; and the corner branch applied to every sample reddens the invariance half - 112 messages against the shipped 160 and an unguarded 256, SO A TEST THAT COUNTED WOULD HAVE GONE GREEN ON IT, which is 11-08's lesson in its own words. The continuous morph is pinned BYTE-IDENTICAL against a 160-pair literal captured against HEAD's morph.ts before the change. ONE DEVIATION WAS A REAL BUG THIS WORK CREATED: 11-09's amplitude probe held x = y = 64, which is cell 40 exactly, so at the middle depth it was pressing the new stop button - its x moved to 40 and the collision is written down where it happened, as the documented hole arriving as a measurement rather than as a claim. Costs re-measured at the RGB444 PICKER corner: ARC 390 -> 523 Setup and 262 -> 275 Timer (385 / 633 free), MORPH 579 -> 710 Setup with the Timer still the empty string and no keeper added (198 free); both headers were ALREADY CORRECT about their corner, both by the same accident of declaring 255,255,255, so the confirmed-wrong count stays at FIVE and thirteen headers remain unchecked for 11-16. NO KNOB AND NO KNOB VALUE MOVED ON EITHER ENTRY - both shape characters are "5" before and after, proved by swapping each entry to its HEAD version and back with sha256 compared either side - so the tripwire POMODORO spent in 11-09 was NOT spent again. 84 files / 858 tests + 1 todo (PREV_FILES +0, PREV_TESTS +2, both in lua-smoke.spec.ts 14 -> 16, check-counts exit 0), sweep 4 19 exit 0 with ALL FOUR TOTALS UNMOVED (compiler Pass A 20,782 / 45,358, Lua Pass A 49,824 / 168,608, lua-entries 1,176, kind cross-product worst 906 of 908 - a second independent confirmation of 11-09's finding that the compiler route cannot move for a hand-authored Lua entry), check 580 with 0 ERRORS 0 WARNINGS, lint clean, e2e grep still 86 and the suite NOT RUN. frames.json BYTE-UNTOUCHED AND PROVED rather than asserted - frames.spec.ts green WITHOUT UPDATE_FRAMES and git diff --quiet exit 0 - with the reason stated per entry: ARC could have moved it and did not because its paint loop is byte-unchanged and F is reachable only from touch_cb, and MORPH could not have because its whole change is inside touch_cb and it has no Timer. Previous status, retained (11-09): THE STATE THAT WAS DESCRIBED, SHIPPED IN THE COPY AND NEVER BUILT, AND A BUDGET CORNER TWO DOCUMENTS QUOTED WRONGLY. STAGE now has a THIRD zone state - the scene you are lining up - and it was never broken: listing.ts:371 has promised "the live one glows and the one you are lining up breathes" since Phase 9 while stage.ts carried only live (rate 4) and under-a-finger (24). Authoring work with a budget cost, not a repair. Z(z,f) became Z(z,f,g) and takes TWO INDEPENDENT AXES because the third state is a cross of the other two - f is the layer-1 rate, g is the layer-2 base - so the lined-up box is the IDLE box with @LIVEC pulsing through it, and its floor is the idle floor EXACTLY, which is the half of the design that says not on air. Measured over 512 ticks at the defaults: idle 248 constant, lined up 248..836 at 20 cycles, live 580..1168 at 8 (48 under a finger); 10 is the geometric mean of 4 and 24, so the middle state is as distinguishable from the slow one as from the fast one. "Nothing is lined up" is spelled self.p == self.l rather than with a sentinel, which makes CUTTING TO THE LINED-UP SCENE clear the line-up for free and removes the Timer's guard entirely. THE GESTURE WAS COSTED FOUR WAYS AND THREE WERE REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT, so the one that ships is a FOURTH the plan never named. The tap-versus-hold discrimination the card already computes is free in characters and is EXACTLY THE DISCRIMINATION THE CLASS-B GATE FORBIDS: gating the gks on e==4 reddens lua-smoke test 5 at "fast tap sent 0 message(s), slow tap sent 1", and whether one quick tap arrives as 4-then-5 or as a coalesced 9 is sub-cycle timing the operator cannot control - a cut that sometimes does not cut. A second contact IS DELIVERABLE, measured on both halves the plan asked for - touch_cb sees i = 0, 1, 2 and TouchSampler allocates slots 0, 1, 2 of five - and STILL FAILS, on a reason the plan did not name: A MOUSE HAS EXACTLY ONE POINTER, so the gesture is invisible in the preview, and the multi-touch audience that could perform it is the audience Web Serial cannot reach at all. A long press fails because STAGE's only clock is a Timer PINNED AT 2560 MS, so its cheapest threshold is 2.56 s with 2.56 s of jitter. WHAT SHIPS IS THE SLIDE: event code 1 reached NO branch of this handler before, so lining up costs the cut path nothing and an installed card behaves as it always did. STAGE'S HEADER QUOTED 511 / 397 - the DECLARED-palette corner - and the plan quoted 505 / 399 free, which is the DEFAULTS and does not even agree with itself (908 - 505 is 403, and 399 free is a Setup of 509, none of the four corners). Costed against 390 free, leaves 255: +135 Setup, +27 Timer. THE TIMER HAD TO GROW A LOOP AND THAT IS THE ONE PLACE THE THIRD STATE IS NOT FREE: 30000 is 300 seconds, so a scene lined up and left five minutes would stop breathing; R(z) is called for both zones and did NOT gain a glpfs. NO KNOB WAS ADDED - STAGE's shape character is c before and after, no knob code line is in the diff, and the tripwire this plan spent ONCE on POMODORO (n -> p, demoting the one wild-stamps payload xn33333 to older, affordable only because the site has never been public) was not spent again for a colour nobody asked for. listing.ts is DELIBERATELY unchanged and the header says why in a line. Task 01 shipped ahead of the pause: ARC's heart is scaled by the same s.d its CC is, +9 characters and not the +21 the plan costed, with the bottom edge now dark and still exactly when the card goes quiet; POMODORO carries six intervals with 1 and 5 APPENDED, its worst corner unmoved at 743/659 because the corner takes the LONGEST value, and four payload literals captured before the change (wn0f2145f31/15, wn1f2145f31/20, wn2f2145f31/25, wn3f2145f31/50) prove indices 0..3 unmoved. The 655 s ceiling belongs to the LONG intervals, not the new short ones. THE CHECKPOINT IS ANSWERED and THREE OF THE FOUR ANSWERS FELL OUTSIDE THEIR OWN OPTION SET: STAGE -> stage-third-state (here), ARC -> a stop/resume FEATURE (11-09.1 task 01, and it CLOSES the worry that the simulator was lying), MORPH -> one tap one message (11-09.1 task 02, cheaper than all three costed because self.k already declares the four corners), LUMEN -> an investigation into whether depth reaches the LEDs at all (11-09.2). MORPH's three clauses stay reported separately against 11-02, 11-08 and 11-09.1. The new test asserts SEPARATION between cycle counts and never a rate literal, plus that LINING UP SENDS NO KEYSTROKE. Previous status, retained (11-08.1): THE PADS THAT WENT BLACK AND NEVER CAME BACK, AND A HARNESS THAT COULD NOT SAY WHY. Before this plan `grep -rn "contextlost\|contextrestored\|isContextLost" src/` returned NOTHING: BrowseGrid builds a card's engine exactly once ever, register() took the 2D context once and cached it, and paint() wrote through it forever - so an engine that drops a backing store, which is what WebKit does with 27 canvas layers on /browse/, blanked a pad for the rest of the visit on the one screen an iOS visitor has. It is now 16 matches in host.ts and 16 in host.spec.ts. BOTH HALVES SHIP AND BOTH ARE MEASURED LOAD-BEARING IN A BROWSER: removing the contextlost/contextrestored pair reddens the STILL branch in chromium AND webkit at Expected 81 Received 0 - the black pad, named - and removing the paint-time isContextLost re-acquire guard reddens the ANIMATING branch in both at arc Expected > 0 Received 0 with its engine still running. The two-halves table saying what each CANNOT catch is in host.ts's header so neither is later deleted as duplication. NO preventDefault(): the canvas-2D polarity is INVERTED from WebGL's - the HTML standard fires a cancelable contextlost and aborts restoration when it comes back canceled - so copying the WebGL idiom from memory would have shipped a listener that runs and achieves nothing; defaultPrevented is asserted false after our listener in both engines. isContextLost NEEDED NEITHER A CAST NOR A LOCAL STRUCTURAL TYPE - TypeScript 6.0.3's lib.dom declares it on the CanvasState mixin - but it IS feature-detected, because it ships in Chrome 130+ / Firefox 151+ and not in the Chrome 89 baseline. THE SCRATCH CLEAR'S CHECK CAME BACK GREEN and is recorded as a finding rather than reconciled: carrying entry.scratch across the loss left 21 of 21 passing, so an ImageData minted by a dead context is fine and the clear is DEFENSIVE, kept with that label. WHETHER WebKit EMITS THESE EVENTS AT ALL IS A NAMED UNKNOWN FOR 11-16, not a claim in either direction: a capturing document listener counted engine-fired (isTrusted) contextlost 0 and contextrestored 0 across the full run, which is "not observed" and nothing more - and it is exactly why the paint guard is not optional. THE VACUOUS VERSION OF THE BROWSER PROOF IS NAMED AND AVOIDED: a synthetic contextlost loses nothing, so dispatch-then-assert-unchanged passes against the unfixed code; the test dispatches, CLEARS THE 9x9 STORE FROM THE TEST, asserts the clear landed at 0 of 81, and only then restores - ninepads 81 -> 0 -> 81 at DOM index 7 with its box on screen asserted unmoved, and arc back at 74 after a drop with NO event at all. BrowseGrid's started set was never consulted and is byte-untouched. THE PERFORMANCE WIN TRADED AWAY AN ACCIDENTAL RESILIENCE: the vendored host takes its context inside blit() on every paint and so never holds a reference across a loss; host.ts caches the context and a persistent ImageData because putImageData into a 9x9 store is the whole point of the faster painter. No vendored byte moves and no manifest row is declared - pad-sim-host appears 18 times outside src/vendor and ZERO of them is an import. EXPECT.POLL RETRIES A FAILING MATCHER AND NOT A THROWING CALLBACK, read out of the shipped invokePollMatcher rather than the docs, and all eleven sites in e2e/ had the hole. MEASURED: unguarded, a planted throw killed the test at 1.7s with an unattributed stack; guarded, it polled its whole 29.7s and named "planted" at an attributable assertion. Previous status, retained (11-07): CONSOLE'S THREE ASKS SHIPPED, AND THE TWO BENCH NOTES THAT HAD REACHED NOTHING ARE ANSWERED WITH MEASUREMENTS RATHER THAN SILENCE. The clamp complaint was arithmetic, not event handling: CONSOLE's fader body is rows 1..8, so h = 8-r runs 0..7 and h*127//8 tops out at 7*127//8 = 111. Swept through the real Lua host, EVERY column emitted exactly 0, 15, 31, 47, 63, 79, 95, 111 and reached 127 nowhere; //7 fixes it at +0 at BOTH sites, and the grep is published rather than the sentence trusted - two Lua sites, both moved, the fader body and the unmute restore, each caught by a DIFFERENT test when reverted alone. After: 0, 18, 36, 54, 72, 90, 108, 127. MUTED FADERS ARE INERT, which REVERSES the shipped touch-to-unmute decision (s.m[c]=nil, on a stated argument carried in the header) and costs -6 rather than the plan's +5: the early-return shape measures exactly +5 as predicted, and folding the same test into the condition the line already has is 11 characters cheaper for identical behaviour, because the reversal deletes s.m[c]=nil anyway. The only way back is the mute cap, reachable by construction - row 0 of the same column, one cell above the fader, painted in @MUTEC on both layers while held - and BOTH halves are asserted, silent while muted and responsive after, because a mute that cannot be undone is worse than the complaint. THE MUTE ROW ANSWERS A SWIPE at +42, guarded by self.q[i]; four shapes were costed and all four fit (this one 844, 11-08's literal idiom 836, this one without the fader-path clear 833, a swipe that SETS rather than toggles 807), so there is no shortfall to report. IT DELIBERATELY DIVERGES FROM 11-08'S IDIOM AND NOT FOR BUDGET: CONSOLE's outer gate never sees a contact end - code 9, the mute row's primary gesture, is a whole contact in one message and passes e<9 - so 11-08's s.q[i]=nil clear would have no site that ever runs and the SECOND fast tap on a cap would be swallowed. Letting the onset pass unconditionally needs no contact-end branch at all, costs eight characters more, and its reason is one sentence in the entry header. Measured unguarded: one still finger toggled a mute 210 TIMES IN 210 SAMPLES and a swipe toggled each column 14-15 times; guarded, 1 and 1, with every probe sample a DISTINCT coordinate inside the cell because the host's enqueue is change-gated and an identical re-send would have measured the host's dedup and called it the entry's. FORGE AND LATTICE WERE SWEPT THE WAY CONSOLE WAS - 16,384 presses each, every raw coordinate - AND NEITHER IS A CLAMP, so neither shipped a Lua change and both are NAMED NON-DELIVERIES CARRYING THEIR NUMBERS. FORGE: all twenty-seven macro indices reachable with both endpoints present; column boundaries at raw x 0, 15, 29, 43, 57, 72, 86, 100, 114 (two columns 15 wide, seven 14) and bands at y 0, 43, 86 (43, 43, 42); target areas 588 to 645 raw units EXCEPT macro 26's 392, two thirds of its band-mates, because cell 80 is the bank corner and returns before the send; the corner itself 196 units sending nothing; and THE PRESS IS FINAL - down at x=10 slid to x=40 emits ONE keystroke, column 0's, and down at y=40 slid to y=90 emits ONE, band 0's. That is the one plausible mechanism for waay not precise enough, and it is NOT fixed here because firing on MOVE would spray keystrokes off a macro pad; committing on release is costed in prose and handed to the bench. LATTICE: 49 distinct notes, 36 to 84, EXACTLY the range its own arithmetic claims, both ends present, every one of the 16,384 coordinates sounding something, cell boundaries identical to the picture's - there is no clamp to improve, and the third clause of that bench note has now been looked at. SIX THINGS THE PLAN ASSERTS THAT THE TREE DOES NOT SUPPORT, and the first is a NEW class of staleness: CONSOLE has 100 FREE AT WORST, NOT 121 - the binding corner is the PICKER corner D-06 opens (255,255,255), 808, not the declared-palette corner the entry header quotes at 789, and the plan's 787 is not any corner this repository measures. Previous status, retained (11-04): THE PERMISSION D-02 GRANTED, SPENT - ONCE, NARROWLY, AND ON THE RECORD, AND NOT WHERE THE PLAN AIMED IT. Class A: the comet family emits (256 - rate) * ticks as the decay start in _pad.ts and pad-sim.ts alike, at seven sites whose line numbers were all exactly where the plan said, and +0 characters at every one of TWELVE reachable trailMs values - re-derived from DECAY_TABLE rather than read off the plan's table, with the freeze moving from 1..7 of 255 to 0 on every row. That is AURORA's stuck red glow and STARFIELD's stuck colour, and it was one expression. Class B: THE PLAN TOLD THIS WAVE TO FIX ENDED AND EXCLUDE tpad BY NAME, AND ENDED IS CORRECT AS WRITTEN. Code 9 IS an end - a press and a lift in one message - and all ten ENDED sites are release paths, so the plan's fix would have left every one of them armed forever; the plan noticed this at exactly one site and carved that site out instead of generalising. What a fast tap loses is the PRESS half, so LIVE became e~=3 and e<5 or e>8 (+7, precedence measured through the real Lua host against the bracketed form before the shorter was chosen) and phaseCond's HELD arm gained or e>8 (+7). tpad therefore reaches NEITHER changed site, none of its bytes moved, and the exclusion was never needed - the trap is avoided by construction. Both of its reasons are recorded anyway because either alone would have sufficed and a reader who sees only the budget one will think the fix gets affordable at a bigger budget: it does not, reason 1 is a correctness bar. The PRESS arm carries the same defect and was deliberately left alone - no reachable HANGAR state compiles it, and an unreachable divergence is a cost a GPLv3 record should not carry. Measured across all nine compiled presets in a real Lua VM before and after, with the BEFORE column re-derived in the resumed session WITHOUT editing the tree by inverting the two guards in the emitted minified Lua and ASSERTING the replacement count, because wave 2 lost three attempts to a String.replace that patched a comment: radar 0/2 DIFFER becomes 2/2 AGREE, joystick 2/4 becomes 4/4, faders 0/1 becomes 1/1, pinwheel's perFinger layer record goes from 0/0/0 - no trail at all - to a live one, and tpad was 6/6 AGREE throughout. NINEPADS IS A FINDING, NOT A FIX: 0 against 2 before and after, because the zones emitter runs if ENDED then z=nil end and the correct repair is note-on AND note-off in one callback, a SHAPE change D-02 does not grant. Declared costs moved +7 at pinwheel 312, radar 445, joystick 542 and faders 520, byte-exact in pad.test.js; no preset was trimmed, no site was wrong-and-unaffordable, sweep over budget 0 across 1,296 combinations. The record is twenty-two intendedDivergence rows across four vendored files against seven predicted hunks - 11-03's finding 5 called this - each matching EXACTLY ONCE, checked directly against the files rather than delegated to the spec because pad-sim.ts:987 and :1002 are byte-identical lines. THE BOUNDARY WAS PROVED, NOT PROMISED: the phase-walk clamp was planted in this session and firmware-oracle.spec.ts went red at exit 1 with EXACTLY ONE test failing - the seventh, agrees on the tick order at layer expiry - and the message named the frozen-frame index (expiry tick 42), not a phase value, because that test's only ticking layer IS the comet layer this plan edits and every assertion is relative to an observed expiry rather than an absolute tick.
