The header says so in those words and the question is deferred item 1, which opened .planning/phases/09-twenty-configurations/deferred-items.md
- [Phase 09]: WHAT THE BROWSER RECORDS IS NOT ALWAYS WHAT THE WIRE CARRIES (09-05). self:gms with mode 1 becomes two CC messages on the module and exactly one HostMidi in HANGAR (lua-host.ts:556-572), so STRIP's test asserts the observable - one call, mode 1, p1 in 0..31 - and the pair is bench row 20. The entry header, the SUMMARY and the row each say which of the two they are talking about
- [Phase 09]: A HEADER CLAIM MUST NOT COUNT ITSELF (09-05). Two entry headers claimed a grep -c over their own FILE, and both were false the moment the header named the number it was warning about. The measurable claim is over the SETUP string: all three of this wave's entries hold zero 65535, zero glt, zero glf and zero glpfs, and STRIP holds exactly one 16383, the clamp
- [Phase 09]: SET THE PHASES ONCE, REPAINT THE COLOURS (09-05). STRIP and LEARN both redraw all 81 cells on a change and both are affordable only because every cell's phase is written to 255 in Setup and never again, so the redraw is glc alone
- [Phase 09]: THE PINNED MINIFIER RESPACES `&` AND `^` AND LEAVES `>>` ALONE (09-07). `(g>>k)&1` compresses to `(g>>k)& 1` and `2^k` to `2 ^ k`, so neither is a fixed point of compressScript and `lua-entries.sweep.spec.ts` test 1 goes red on a stored one; `>>` is returned byte for byte. `^` is disqualified twice over, because it also returns a FLOAT in Lua 5.4 and a fraction passed to a firmware call silently becomes 0. Both bit-masked entries in the catalog store `(mask >> bit) % 2`, and `%` binds tighter than `>>` so the parentheses are load-bearing.
- [Phase 09]: A ONE-SHOT TIMER ARMED BY A GESTURE IS NOT AN ANIMATION (09-07). `lua-host.ts:604-618` consumes the deadline on every fire and only the body's own `gtt` re-arms it, so a Timer that Setup never arms and that re-arms only under a condition leaves `timerArmed` FALSE at rest. That is how FORGE ships the watchdog `ZONA-CAPABILITIES.md` section 6 point 5 requires and is still honestly `static`; the alternative was a card reporting `animating` at every sampled tick on a pad where nothing moves.
- [Phase 09]: RELEASE A HELD MODE ON THE CONTACT ID, NEVER ON THE CELL (09-07). Press the corner, drift off it, lift is the commonest real gesture there is and the lift arrives with coordinates somewhere else. FORGE stores the id that armed the bank and releases on any end event for that id; observed at (10,10), far from the corner it armed on.
- [Phase 09]: A SECOND MODIFIER IS A SAFER SECOND BANK THAN A KEYCODE SHIFT (09-07). A shift has to land somewhere for every base key, and from F13 twenty-seven macros already reach usage id 130, so any offset walks into the LANG and system ids. Shift plus the same key cannot land on an id nobody chose — and it forces left shift OUT of the base modifier's value list, because otherwise the two banks send the same chord.
- [Phase 09]: A FRAME HASH IS A CHANGE DETECTOR, NOT A DISTINCTNESS GATE (09-07). Two of SWITCH's nine glyphs were made identical; only `frames.spec.ts` test 3 went red and only on the hash, and with the fixture re-taken the whole quick suite was 74 files / 780 passed with a card promising nine marks and drawing eight. Deferred item 3 records what would close it.
- [Phase 09]: A SINGLE SIGNED INDEX STEP CANNOT TORUS-WRAP A 9x9 GRID (09-08). The row component is `step//9`, which Lua floor-divides to MINUS ONE for a step of -1, so a left-moving snake climbs a row every step and nothing looks wrong. Two signed fields and `(h//9+v)%9*9+(h%9+u)%9` wrap both axes in one expression, and Lua's floor-modulo turns -1 into 8 for free
- [Phase 09]: A DETERMINISTIC CARD THAT PLAYS ITSELF NEEDS A REASON TO TURN (09-08). SNAKE as planned travels in a straight line forever, circles one row on the torus, never eats twice and never dies — a still picture that happens to move. A perpendicular-only greedy autopilot costs 121 characters, cannot reverse by construction, and produces five bites and a death every 6.5 seconds, identically, forever
- [Phase 09]: `glp` AFTER A DECAY IS A BUG, NOT A REPAINT (09-08). It writes `pha` and leaves `fre` alone by design, so a cell handed rate 247 by a dying trail and then lifted to phase 255 fades straight back out. `glpfs(a,l,255,0,0)` is eight characters more and is the correct call
- [Phase 09]: A NOTE CEILING IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A KNOB (09-08). 256-byte buffer, 14 bytes a voice message, about eighteen a cycle, and an append that does not fit is refused WITH NO ERROR. LIFE caps births at the literal 6 — twelve messages, 168 bytes — because a visitor who raised it would lose notes silently
- [Phase 09]: A PERSISTENT MARK IS A CELL WITH NO TIMEOUT, AND THE MISSING CALL NEEDS A DO-NOT-ADD (09-08). Every touch look on the compiler's sheet is a self-erasing decay, so ETCH's whole card is the `glt` that is not there; without the note in the header, adding one reads as a fix
- [Phase 09]: `RECORDED.singletons` COUNTS TAGS, NOT ENTRIES (09-07). It counts tags carried by exactly one entry, and it happened to equal the entry count at twenty-five and at twenty-eight — 51 tags minus 23 chips is 28. At thirty-one entries it is 29, and `filter.spec.ts` test 5 said so by name on the first run.
- [Phase 09]: D-08's move is a git mv and nothing else. The shipped file-name rule already routes the sweep glob, so vite.config.ts is unedited and the history follows the file. Measured honestly: the quick run did NOT get faster (it is parallel and lua-entries was never its critical path) - what the move buys is headroom against the memory-pressure timeout, and the sweep went 110 s -> 87 s despite gaining a file (09-01)
- [Phase 07]: A RESERVED CELL SIZES ON THE TALLEST OF ALL ITS STRINGS AT THE PANEL'S ACTUAL WIDTH, and the floor is the backstop, not the mechanism: PutBack's line cell holds all three candidate strings at grid-area 1 / 1 (the inactive two visibility: hidden and aria-hidden) with a 72px floor derived at 372px, and measured 72 in every state at 372px and 96 in every state at 280px - a dropped twin is invisible at 372 (the floor masks it) and a 24px jump under the destructive control at 280, which is why the negative was observed at the narrower column
- [Phase 07]: THE CONFIRMATION IS A GROUP WITH A PROGRAMMATIC FOCUS TARGET, NOT A DIALOG AND NOT A TRAP: KeepConfirm takes focus on its container so the PERMANENT caption and the naming sentence are what assistive technology reads first and no key press commits without a Tab; Tab walks out in DOM order; focus leaving does NOT close a pending decision (unlike Phase 6's disclosure, which is reading material); Escape is handled at the window and acts only while focus is inside; the affirmative is a bordered secondary, never the accent fill, because a second primary at the moment of the irreversible thing would invert SAFE-02
- [Phase 07]: A STRUCTURAL FLOOR RULE READS EVERY DECLARATION: device-ui.spec.ts test 8 collects every min-block-size on a control's class and requires each to be 44px, after the plan's `40px on NOT NOW` mutation stayed green in its additive form under a presence-only check - a rule a later declaration can silently override is not a floor; and a quick-suite failure in a file a plan does not touch (Phase 8's lua-entries.spec.ts timing out under load, passing alone) is recorded and deferred after the one permitted rerun, never rerun until green and never fixed out of scope
- [Phase 07]: A PROMISE IS RETIRED BY NAME, AND A SCAN KEEPS IT RETIRED: TryOnDevice's HONESTY, IDENTIFIED_REASON and IDENTIFIED_TAIL were deleted with their identifiers and their last text recorded in 07-10-SUMMARY.md (D-18's second edit), and device-ui.spec.ts test 10 holds the comment-stripped file free of the fragments `never writes` and `next release` so the sentence cannot come back under another constant; the honesty slot's precedence now puts the capability sentence first (Z-06), amending Phase 6, because the standing line promises a write and on an incapable browser that would be a lie.
- [Phase 07]: A BUSY LABEL RIDES THE CONTROL THAT WAS CLICKED UNLESS THAT CONTROL HAS LEFT THE SCREEN: WRITING… on the primary for its own RAM leg; KEEPING… on the primary for the confirmation's store leg because the affirmative unmounts on the commit and the row's KEEP ON DEVICE returns as a quiet disabled control that cannot carry it; the KEEP cell holds its last non-writing line through the leg (I3 rule 4); the swap has no transition by construction and the flash confirmation leaves instantly by decision (item 11), because a leaving fade would put keep-confirm-yes and keep-on-device on the screen together for 160 ms.
- [Phase 07]: A RECORD LEFT BY AN INTERRUPTED SESSION IS RECONCILED AGAINST THE SERVED BUILD, NOT ACCEPTED: 07-10's three task commits were treated as candidates - each task's verify block run on the committed code, every plan measurement re-observed on a clean build served on 4174 through the shim and the Node responder, and the negative checks with no evidence in any commit (the retained literal on screen, the dropped writing guard on Escape, the space-between row) performed on mutated builds with each restore hashed against HEAD - before the SUMMARY was written; a commit message is a claim, a measurement is evidence, and an uncommitted deferred item is kept only once it is checked against the code.
- [Phase 07]: THE HEADER'S CONTRIBUTION TO A WRITE IS A LOCK, NOT A LABEL (Z-15): DISCONNECT ZONA and FORGET THIS ZONA carry disabled={session.writeLock} - the session's flag, set and cleared by the install store around every leg - with WRITE_LOCK_REASON beneath the pair as their aria-describedby target, bound only while the line is rendered; the component reads no leg and device-ui.spec.ts test 11 holds `install.leg` absent from it, because the RAM-legs-only mutation released both controls over the store leg at +9 ms on a served build with the reason line still on screen (07-11).
- [Phase 07]: A SENTENCE ABOUT A STORED FACT RENDERS ONLY WHILE THE FACT EXISTS AND PICKS ITS FORM FROM THE STORE THAT OWNS IT: the S4 snapshot line is gated on install.snapshot and chooses between the durable and session-only forms on install.snapshotDurable - observed as no line for the 35 ms snapshotting gap and the durable form after it, and no line for the 1.3 s a serial-less module spent failing SERIALNUMBER/FETCH three times and the honest form after that; the panel's DISCONNECT ZONA keeps its protective-only disabled (item 14 decided as item 17: I3 rule 4 forbids region 3 moving mid-write, the region is aria-busy for the leg, DEGR-02's letter is about a permanent capability) (07-11).
- [Phase 07]: A DEGRADE TEST IS EXTENDED BY INSERTION BEFORE ITS CONSOLE CHECK, its shipped assertions byte-identical and its title unchanged so the suite total does not move; and a pre-hydration key race is fixed at every site with the same mechanism, not only the ones an item named - the one-line data-ready wait went in at three (07-11).
- [Phase 07]: ON THE REAL PAGE THE HEADER LOCK IS MET ONLY AFTER AN UN-CHOOSE DURING A LEG, because panelOwnsProse is page.state.chosen and the drawer never renders while the panel that holds every writing control is open; the measurements reached it the way a visitor could - history.back() inside the leg, since Escape is refused while writing (Z-10) and Back is not - and that asymmetry is deferred item 19 for the spec's owner rather than a change made here (07-11).
- [Phase 07]: A RAM LEG IS HELD 200 MS PER ACKNOWLEDGEMENT AND THE SLOW LINE IS OBSERVED ON A STORE LEG (07-12): the request id is minted per attempt, the waiter is armed before the write and delayAckMs stalls the page's write() itself, so a CONFIG hold past executeMs 250 is stale on arrival; both events landed on attempt 1 in every run, WRITING… was caught at +39 to +47 ms and the header lock at +94 to +141 ms, and without the hold the lock assertion was red 3 of 3 while the label was still caught.
- [Phase 07]: A STORE LEG'S PROOF IS PACED BY A BOUNDED BEAT LOOP, NEVER ONE TIMED BEAT (07-12): the acknowledgement's landing is invisible from the page and the heartbeat waiter is armed only after it, so the loop pushes a beat, polls the panel for about one heartbeat period and pushes again, capped at twelve - the same state needed 1, 2 and 3 beats across runs, which is the flakiness a single beat would have had.
- [Phase 07]: A DEGRADE TEST THAT RUNS ON BOTH PROJECTS ASSERTS WHAT IS TRUE ON BOTH AND NAMES WHAT IS NOT (07-12): the panel adds no horizontal overflow on either project, the document has zero excess at the phone layout, and the desktop's pre-existing one pixel at 1280 is logged, bounded at that pixel and pointed at deferred item 20 - two candidate models were observed red and dropped before this one, and no source file was edited by rule.
- [Phase 07]: A RESERVED CELL IS READ THROUGH ITS VISIBLE TWIN (07-12): every panel cell renders all of its candidate strings at grid-area 1 / 1 with the inactive ones aria-hidden, so toContainText on the cell is true of every string at once; p[aria-hidden="false"] is the one on screen, and the two PUT BACK forms and the seven KEEP strings are distinguishable.
- [Phase 07]: THE HARDWARE HALVES CLOSE AS VERIFIED-BY-USER-PENDING, IN THOSE WORDS (07-13): SAFE-02, SAFE-03, SAFE-04, SAFE-05 and SAFE-06 are closed against the fake with the hardware row that would confirm each named beside it (INSTALL-RUNBOOK rows D, C, A and F, E, G), SAFE-04 with its key wire-unproven so a failed row A closes it as partially verified, and no agent wrote a byte to a real ZONA - a phase that marked the hardware half done because a fake agreed with a source reading would be doing exactly what the fake's own header warns about.
- [Phase 07]: THE RUNBOOK'S ROWS ARE DESIGNED AGAINST THE STORE'S SEMANTICS, NOT TRANSCRIBED FROM THE RESEARCH (07-13): rows A to F run in one tab because keptThisSession is a field of the page load and a fresh tab's PUT BACK is memory-only (item 29); row F leaves a memory-only try-on on the module before the browser is quit, so the pad coming back to the original can only have come from the browser's storage; row E's round count is read from the probe's steps list because refetchRounds has no readout (item 28); row B's pacing answer is deducible from the first click's behaviour on the real page (the escalation fires only on a write timeout with no refusal) and confirmed on the probe.
- [Phase 07]: D-19 IS READ IN ITS AMENDED FORM AND BOTH READINGS ARE ON THE RECORD (07-13): the clause as first written said the write path uses the desktop's 10 ms and the runbook measures 0 ms; what ships is the reverse - 0 ms pre-send (PRE_SEND_DELAY_MS, SKELETON-RESULTS (b)) escalating once to DESKTOP_PRE_SEND_DELAY_MS on a write timeout with zero NACKs (07-RESEARCH Pitfall 3) - and row B records whether the escalation ever fired. The 0 ms-then-escalate design stands; nobody re-inverts it from the older wording.
- [Phase 07]: D-13'S "IN BOTH PROJECTS" MEANS THE INSTALL ON CHROMIUM AND THE DEGRADE ON THE PHONE PROJECT (07-13): the six probe walks and the four real-page titles run on chromium alone, because Web Serial exists in no WebKit, and the one @webkit title (07-12's test 11) is the degrade path; the e2e delta of +12 is six untagged plus four untagged plus one tagged counted twice, not two engines walking the install.
- [Phase 10]: CLEAR writes the firmware's own defaultConfig (Setup 641, Timer 22), read from TOUCH_EVENTS by event number through a defaultFor() that throws at module load - never by array position, which the negative check proved would pass today by coincidence
- [Phase 10]: The canonicity gate lives in constants.spec.ts beside the export it pins, not in protocol-pin.spec.ts as UI-SPEC 10.5 says: 10-VALIDATION lists that file among the standing gates not edited this phase
- [Phase 10]: SAFE-05 is recorded as NOT extended and its REQUIREMENTS.md row left exactly as Phase 7 wrote it - A-45 removed the confirmation the approved ruling extended it with, and its subject is the control that stores to flash while CLEAR writes RAM only
- [Phase 10]: The register line is a line of AUTHORSHIP rather than DOM containment - 19.1g's statement is false against the shipped tree, because FrontDoor.svelte mounts Coverflow.svelte and the whole instrument register is a DOM descendant of .front-door
- [Phase 10]: The second halftone density does NOT ship: 12.00 ms delta at p95 on webkit-phone against a declared 2 ms threshold, measured before any CSS was written
- [Phase 10]: isolation: isolate is load-bearing on the lattice root - without it the lattice was a silent no-op that every source scan passed
- [Phase 10]: CLEAR FILTERS is pilled: A-41 binds the TIER, and 10.3's Secondary row enumerates one panel's controls rather than closing the tier
- [Phase 10]: The metadata block is --font-mono's SEVENTH use (A-44), qualifying on W-03's columns half rather than its jitter half
- [Phase 10]: The lattice is a GROUND: it paints where nothing is standing on it - a relocation, not a knock-back, because an opacity changes the symptom's amplitude and not its cause (A-55)
- [Phase 10]: The ground rule uses :where() so a component's own scoped rule outranks it, and is declared ABOVE .pill because at equal specificity source order decides the tie (A-55)
- [Phase 10]: Three declared exceptions and no fourth; BrowseGrid's .empty was found by a DOM walk, not by reading a template (A-56)
- [Phase 10]: The unlit-cell wash is global rather than scoped to tpad, because scoping it would make the one card that can never light the brightest unlit face on the site (A-58)
- [Phase 10]: instrument.spec.ts's lattice declaration count is a FLOOR, not an equality - the brief expected a number that does not exist (A-60)
- [Phase 10]: No requirement checkbox was ticked at the Phase 10 gate. All twenty owned IDs were closed by earlier phases; CAT-04 stays Pending for the third time and TUNE-04 keeps Phase 5's tick without being re-claimed, because MIX TWO is built and not mounted
- [Phase 10]: The phase total is written as a sixteen-term chain from BASE_TESTS 780 to 828 and a six-term chain from BASE_E2E 89 to 103, so the baseline and the total are visibly the same arithmetic seen from two ends
- [Phase 10]: Twelve of 10-VALIDATION's estimates and instructions are named as wrong rather than silently corrected, including the gate's own negative check (a floor six below its observation) and its SAFE-02 qualifier (four tiers and five channels where the tree has three and three)
- [Phase 11]: D-01 executed as option (a): drums and clips retired, ninepads re-homed to play and stage to shortcuts on how each card feels (CONT-03), landing eight FOR terms every one carrying two or more with the zero-singleton rule unweakened
- [Phase 11]: Both decay idioms rescued verbatim out of life.ts and gridlock.ts into decay-idiom.spec.ts's header before the same plan deleted those two files; gridlock.ts's computed form is the one 11-02 needs for CHORUS and 11-RESEARCH.md named only the other
- [Phase 11]: docs/TESTING.md's dated measurement rows were NOT rewritten to the new catalog size - a measurement edited to match a later tree is no longer a measurement - and carry a dated amendment instead, with 11-16 owning the re-measure
- [Phase 11]: The typed e2e search word moved from drums to colour: drums lived only in the retired tag, so D-01 would have taken e2e/browse.e2e.ts's narrow-without-emptying assertion to zero on the deployed artefact with nothing in the quick run to catch it
- [Phase 11]: 11-02: SONAR and GHOST fixed as 252,250 with glt 42 UNCHANGED - the house idiom at T=42, +0 characters - rather than the plan's 252,247 with glt 28, which would have shortened both trails from 420 ms to 280 ms for no stated reason
- [Phase 11]: 11-02: CHORUS's @BLOOMRATE becomes @SPREAD and stops being a rate. The rate is fixed at 4 so the timeout can derive from the per-cell start; label and help text moved with it. The knob ID and arity did NOT move, because wild-stamps.json holds two CHORUS records keyed on bloomSpeed at indices 1 and 4
- [Phase 11]: 11-02: DECLARED_EXCEPTIONS holds TWO rows, not the plan's one. forge.ts's bare e==4 is the FIX rather than the bug - the onset arms a latch only a lift clears - and each row is keyed on the whole if/elseif BRANCH so the same comparison moved out of its chain stops being excused
- [Phase 11]: 11-03: the byte-pin D-02 dropped is replaced by upstream-manifest.json's intendedDivergence - hunk, reason, plan, date - and the record landed EMPTY, proved by five negative checks before 11-04 spends it
- [Phase 11]: 11-03: the inversion order is divergences-then-deltas (last applied, first inverted), derived from the sync procedure and proved by an added negative check; the plan's own two sentences contradicted each other
- [Phase 11]: 11-03: scripts/record-upstream-manifest.mjs now carries divergence rows forward - as written, VENDOR.md's own regeneration step would have deleted the whole record
- [Phase 11]: 11-03: preset-baseline.spec.ts's INTENDED_DIVERGENCE rows carry BOTH substrings so a declared divergence re-cuts the character comparison rather than suppressing it; D-08 is suspended for the named few and stops for everything else
- [Phase 11]: 11-03: CONT-01 and FOUND-02 recorded for 11-16 with verbatim current text and proposed wording, not amended here; requirements-completed is empty because CONT-01 is made less true by this phase, not more
- [Phase 11]: 11-04: ENDED is correct as written and was NOT changed at any of its ten sites, contradicting the plan. Code 9 IS an end and every ENDED site is a release path; the plan's fix would have left all ten armed forever. The press half was fixed at LIVE and at phaseCond's held arm instead, +7 each.
- [Phase 11]: 11-04: tpad needed no exclusion by name. Because ENDED was not changed, tpad reaches neither modified site and none of its bytes moved; the trap the plan carved around is avoided by construction. Its 902 of 908 (907 at worst knob) is untouched.
- [Phase 11]: 11-04: golden-frames.json and frames.json were NOT regenerated because they do not move. Both samplers construct an engine and run ticks with NO touch input, so a touch-response constant cannot reach the frame. A regeneration would have rewritten two tripwires to identical content.
- [Phase 11]: HANGAR declares the nine preset VALUES in src/lib/catalog/presets.ts over the vendored defaultState/normalisePadState; PadPreset, PadState and KnobKind stay vendored TYPES. The structural blocker behind eight bench notes is removed.
- [Phase 11]: The read-through guarantee is replaced by presets.spec.ts's field-path diff over the union of both objects' keys, with INTENDED_DIVERGENCE empty and the trade stated in the header - and test 2's non-vacuous half asserts that statement is still present.
- [Phase 11]: The two fidelity fixtures and capture-preset-baseline.mjs stay on the vendored shelf; catalog.spec.ts also stays there and its name/description comparison became REAL rather than a tautology on the day ported.ts was flipped.
- [Phase 11]: The plan's declared-cost table is the PRE-11-04 one for the third wave running: measured 415/477/403 against 408/463/396, and the delta is +165 rather than +158 because 11-04's class-B guard lands on the LIVE arm only an enabled sends compiles
- [Phase 11]: JOYSTICK's trail is a FINDING and did not ship: sendsInit emits the power-on lit cell only when springLed returns glow, so touch.kind = comet spends the very thing the same bench note's first clause asks for and the card measures ZERO lit bytes at rest
- [Phase 11]: invertY was NOT flipped alongside springTo, against the plan: springRestCell never reads invert once springTo is centre, so the pairing saves 4 characters and reverses a second decision nobody mentioned
- [Phase 11]: NINE PADS' 4x4 lights 32 bytes against 3x3's 162 - four does not divide nine, so the compiler lights one marker cell per zone instead of tiling. It ships as selectable and both figures are pinned
- [Phase 11]: INTENDED_DIVERGENCE moved out of presets.spec.ts into src/lib/catalog/divergence.ts, because catalog.spec.ts, frames.spec.ts and e2e/fidelity.e2e.ts all hold a HANGAR value against a vendored one and a spec cannot import a spec
- [Phase 11]: 11-04's bloom/disturb rule is now a gate: the planted bloom was caught as UNDECLARED and presets.spec.ts went fully GREEN once a row was written for it and the cost refreshed
- [Phase 11]: CONSOLE has 100 free at the worst knob position, not the plan's 121 and not the header's 119: the binding corner is the RGB444 PICKER corner D-06 opens (808), not the declared-palette corner (789). Every hand-authored entry header written before plan 10-08 quotes the wrong corner; CONSOLE's and FORGE's were corrected, fifteen are unchecked and belong to 11-16.
- [Phase 11]: The muted-fader gate was folded into the existing condition (and not s.m[c]) rather than written as the plan's early return. Measured -6 against +5 for identical behaviour, because the reversal deletes s.m[c]=nil anyway - a correctness change that gives budget back.
- [Phase 11]: CONSOLE's mute-row guard lets the ONSET pass unconditionally, diverging from 11-08's idiom on purpose and not for budget: CONSOLE's outer gate never sees a contact end, so 11-08's contact-end clear would never run and the second fast tap on a cap would be swallowed. 844 against 836.
- [Phase 11]: FORGE shipped NO Lua change. All twenty-seven macros reachable with both endpoints; macro 26's target is 392 raw units against 588-645 because the bank corner takes a third of it; and THE PRESS IS FINAL - a slide across a boundary emits one keystroke, the landing one. Firing on MOVE would spray keystrokes, so the fix is a bench decision handed over with its numbers.
- [Phase 11]: LATTICE shipped NO Lua change. 49 notes, 36 to 84, exactly the range its own arithmetic claims, both ends reachable, all 16,384 coordinates sounding something. The third clause of its bench note - clamp it better - has now been looked at and there is nothing there.
- [Phase 11]: frames.json does not move for a touch-behaviour change, structurally: the fixture samples an untouched pad. Proved by the committed tripwire re-sampling green rather than regenerated to identical content.
- [Phase 11]: No preventDefault() on a canvas-2D contextlost: the polarity is INVERTED from WebGL's and canceling the event is what tells the user agent not to restore (HTML standard, Canvas context lost and restored) - proved with defaultPrevented asserted false after HANGAR's listener in both engines
- [Phase 11]: SimHost notices a dead 2D context TWICE, by listener pair and by paint-time isContextLost re-acquire, and neither is redundant: each was measured RED in a browser where the other is green
- [Phase 11]: The scratch clear across a context loss is DEFENSIVE, not load-bearing - its negative check came back green at 21 of 21 - and it is kept with that label rather than claimed as a check that bites
- [Phase 11]: A negated expect.poll takes e2e/poll.ts's INVERTED guard, never the positive one: a string sentinel satisfies the negation and turns a page that cannot be read at all GREEN, measured at 2.7s against 29.8s red
- [Phase 11]: Counting title openings in e2e source is a sound proof of a ZERO e2e delta and an unsound proof of anything else, because a tagged title counts once in the grep and twice in the run - 11-16 must derive its chain from the run total
- [Phase 11]: STAGE's third zone state is selected by SLIDING onto a zone (event code 1, which reached no branch before), because it is the only candidate that adds a state without reversing an installed card's cut behaviour
- [Phase 11]: The tap-versus-hold gesture is rejected: it is exactly the discrimination the class-B parity gate forbids, measured red at 'fast tap sent 0, slow tap sent 1'
- [Phase 11]: A second contact is rejected despite being deliverable through both the Lua host and the shipped TouchSampler, because a mouse has one pointer and PREV-01 requires the headline gesture to be visible in the preview
- [Phase 11]: A long press is rejected because STAGE's Timer period is pinned at 2560 ms, making 2.56 s the cheapest expressible threshold
- [Phase 11]: Z(z,f) becomes Z(z,f,g) - two independent axes rather than a wider enum - because the lined-up state is the cross of the idle base and the live rate
- [Phase 11]: 'Nothing is lined up' is spelled self.p == self.l rather than with an out-of-range sentinel, so consuming the selection on a cut is free
- [Phase 11]: No knob was added to STAGE, so its shape character stays c and no shared STAGE link is demoted; POMODORO already spent that tripwire once (n -> p)
- [Phase 11]: STAGE's header budget is re-quoted at the RGB444 picker corner (518/109, 390 free before; 653/136, 255 free after), making STAGE the fifth entry confirmed with that defect
- [Phase 11]: ARC's stop is a separate flag multiplied into the phase step, never a zeroed s.r that the next touch would revive
- [Phase 11]: The stopped picture is the frozen swirl, not the frozen heart, because the heart alone reaches phase 0 on 2 of 60 stop moments
- [Phase 11]: A stopped ARC goes on sending 50 messages a second: ARC has one CC, MIDI is stateful, and a silent stop would be indistinguishable from a Timer that raised
- [Phase 11]: MORPH's corner tap is gated on the onset edge, not on a coalesced DOWNUP, because the shipped TouchSampler never produces code 9 at all
- [Phase 11]: One onset idiom, e==4 or e>8, cited to stage.ts and used by both entries in one plan, so the catalogue has one answer to tap-versus-drag
- [Phase 11]: 11-09.2: LUMEN's depth knob delivers to the emitted frame - four values, four distinct 243-byte frames, bottom row 196,69,0 to 27,9,0 - and nothing was shipped to the entry's Lua because {1,2,3,4} is the only four-element set 8*max(@DEPTH) < 36 admits, so the ramp is already at full travel
- [Phase 11]: 11-09.2: the deepening the bench note asked for is reported as a SHORTFALL with two costed routes - move the default index 2 to 3, or move subtrahend and divisor both to 32 for an exact-black bottom row at the same 608 - and left for the user's bench to choose
- [Phase 11]: gmss registered as the sixteenth host global, on its own HostSysex recorder rather than a widened HostHid or HostMidi, and with no call discriminant because gmss is the only sysex emitter in the Grid global table
- [Phase 11]: LUMEN sends its colour as six ASCII hex digits over sysex: the encoding decided by the seven-bit data-byte rule, since row 0 of that pad asks for 255 and a raw RGB payload could not carry it
- [Phase 11]: The Setup event carries the sysex send, gated on the cursor changing or a contact beginning; LUMEN's Timer stays entirely unspent because a per-tick send is a different card
- [Phase 11]: LUMEN's x*127//128 fixed on both axes at minus eighteen characters, because the entry never calls txma/tyma and the scale had nothing to scale
- [Phase 11]: GHOST's reset is ONE lit red corner cell (screen 80), chosen over a held press and a two-cell corner after all three were costed as Lua: a hold has no affordance, so it answers 'resetting is not reliable' with 'and now you must hold it for the right length of time'
- [Phase 11]: The erase key exists exactly while it is lit, so the pad never has a dead square and the marker doubles as the state indicator - lit means a ghost is looping
- [Phase 11]: The clear is glpfs(a,l,0,0,0) - phase 0 AND rate 0 - because glp(a,l,0) leaves the rate running and a cell mid-decay is back at phase 250 on the next tick; measured, the old idiom never clears at all
- [Phase 11]: The erase marker PULSES rather than sitting steady, proved load-bearing by planting the steady form and reading the residue probe report cell 80 at phase 252
- [Phase 11]: 11-12: SHUTTLE LATCHES, reversing shuttle.ts:79-89's documented refusal, because the objection was never to latching but to a latch whose only exit is an unmarked centre column - the stop is now the whole bottom row painted red, and dark whenever there is nothing to stop
- [Phase 11]: 11-12: NO TAP-VERSUS-HOLD RULE, and that is the answer to the second documented reason - the exit is a press, so a coalesced code-9 tap stops the transport in one message and no branch reads contact duration
- [Phase 11]: 11-12: the repaint moved from touch_cb into the Timer because the latching design was 27 characters OVER 908 on the Setup while the Timer had 727 free - a shortfall reported to the architecture instead of trimmed from the ask
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: TWO CONTROLS, NOT THREE - 'two faders, one crossfader at the bottom and the big one' is a count plus a two-member apposition naming what was counted, and the three-control reading contradicts its own 'two faders' while leaving 'the big one' without a referent
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: the rejected three-control reading was PRICED rather than argued away - 1,088 of 908 at the picker corner, 180 over, and it can only be made to fit by moving the repaint into a Timer, which reclassifies a card of static faders as animated
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: every gesture is locked at its onset to the control it started on (s.o[i]), at 61 characters, because two controls that share an edge otherwise interfere whatever controller numbers they send on
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: the ten-bit unlock survives and the criterion is AXIS OWNERSHIP, not continuity - it buys a control that owns a whole axis nothing (the crossfader sends x//8, exactly what a locked axis reports) and buys the fader back the 14 codes its missing ninth row took
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: both axes are unlocked though only y needs it, because lua-host.ts keeps ONE _coordMax for both and an entry unlocking y alone would be simulated with a ten-bit x it does not have on the module (D-11-13-a)
- [Phase 11]: 11-13: the fader's value is clamped with glim after a probe found sixteen NEGATIVE controller values on the wire with every gate in the tree green - the bug the origin lock itself creates
- [Phase 11]: 11-15: a stored Timer does NOT make an entry animated - it is Setup arming it and the body re-arming unconditionally that does, and FORGE has shipped the counter-example since 09-07. WHEELS declares static and the fixture confirms it at all five ticks.
- [Phase 11]: 11-15: the ten-bit unlock has a THIRD justification beside 11-13's two - a control whose OUTPUT IS WIDER THAN THE AXIS. Pitch owns the whole y axis and still wants it, because 128 raw positions into a 14-bit value is a 7-bit controller wearing a 14-bit label.
- [Phase 11]: D-11-15-a: host-surface.spec.ts cannot express an entry-installed self: method, though self.touch_cb proves the construct is legal and universal. self.D ran green in a live host and was withdrawn rather than widening a safety gate inside a wave authoring a card the user will flash.
- [Phase 11]: 11-14: the checkpoint answered new-entry - the RADAR preset stays at ring position 5 untouched and RADAR POINTS carries the radar ask as a second hand-authored card beside it and beside SONAR, with SONAR reused character for character and the one geometric difference the two names imply: the ring is the time, the direction the pitch
- [Phase 11]: 11-14: an eight-entry scale table for eight compass directions was withdrawn on the tune panel's word table (view.ts SCALE_WORDS, derived from the catalog), not on cost - the directions walk a named scale and wrap an octave instead, at nineteen more characters
- [Phase 11]: 11-14: the id is the plan's placeholder radar-points and the name is RADAR POINTS, the first two-word hand-authored name; the audition's name parser was widened by name to read it rather than the name bent to the parser

- [Phase 12]: 12-01: NINE PADS at 4x4 lands 556, where presets.ts:257-263 and 12-RESEARCH.md both declare 550. Recorded, not reconciled - 12-05 re-measures at the picker corner when it moves the default
- [Phase 12]: 12-02: the element is the LAST parameter of fetchConfig and sendConfig - beside `event` is impossible for sendConfig because `config` already sits there, so an element before it would retarget every existing five-argument call at a string coerced to a number
- [Phase 12]: 12-02: the synthetic ZONA takes a SECOND MAP, not a re-key - `system` and `systemFlash` beside `configs` and `flash`, all keyed by event number, because the moduleState factory's eighteen call sites and install.spec.ts's toEqual on a two-key flash already pass that key
- [Phase 12]: 12-02: STEP_IDS gained three ids and FetchedEvent.label gained "System" - neither named by the plan; the first because the step id is a typed union q.request takes, the second so a write-guard refusal names the slot that failed
- [Phase 12]: 12-02: the three tasks were committed 01, 03, 02 - task 02's test needs task 03's fixture, as the plan's own words admit, so plan order would have made the middle commit red
- [Phase 12]: 12-03: THE TUNER DOES NOT REACH THE PROTOCOL, and the plan's mechanism for it does not exist. model.ts has no lazy protocol import and ladder.spec.ts:275 forbids adding one (it went RED). The tuner publishes system verbatim from a new systemSetup option, empty when the entry has none, and install.svelte.ts substitutes SYSTEM_DEFAULT_SETUP in ONE function used by the write path and by armed alike
- [Phase 12]: 12-03: CLEAR RESETS BOTH ELEMENTS (research option A). Under option B a KEEP after a CLEAR would store HANGAR's library to flash and D-21's 41-character line would be untrue by one element on a page HANGAR did write. Cost: one acknowledgement. Bonus: writing an event its own default sets cfg_default_flag and the page store DELETES the cfg, so a KEEP after a CLEAR leaves no HANGAR file on the module at all
- [Phase 12]: 12-03: A V1 SNAPSHOT ENTRY BLOCKS A V2 ENTRY FOR ITS OWN PAGE, which the plan does not ask for. readSnapshot reads v2 first, so a v2 entry written beside a v1 one SHADOWS the original - the same loss with a longer name. persistIfAbsent returns kept and both records stay byte-unchanged
- [Phase 12]: 12-03: THE COUNTS ARE DERIVED FROM STEP LINES, NOT MULTIPLIED. Twenty-two numeric sites in install.e2e.ts: twenty moved, :644 and :670 proved unchanged, and :613 landed on FIVE - the negative check that set it to the blanket six went red with Received: 5. Three more sites the plan's table does not name were found by reading and by the first suite run, one of them a rendered readout string that no numeric grep can see
- [Phase 12]: 12-04: THE CATALOG IS 26 (9 preset + 17 Lua). LATTICE, FORGE and SHUTTLE removed on the second bench round - SHUTTLE despite 11-12 having rebuilt it from a blank page. D-01 APPLIED A SECOND TIME AND NEITHER FACET RULE WEAKENED: keys fell to one carrier and was RETIRED (CHORUS to play), still fell to five and was RE-HOMED back to six (LUMEN to still). LUMEN on still was TAKEN, not stopped on: the axis facets.ts declares is 'moves by itself', LUMEN has no Timer at all, every other carrier of still is a control that moves under a hand, and the entry's own quiet line already read 'stays lit and still'. LEGACY_TAG_MAP's harmonic re-targeted to play - and the plan's expected carrier is WRONG: 09-04-SUMMARY.md:597 records harmonic's two carriers as CHORUS and the ENTRY called KEYS (deleted by 11-01), not LATTICE, so the row is folded twice and the comment says so. RETIRED_VOCABULARY is byte-identical and the five 55 sites are proved unmoved by diff; no keys row was added, because keys was minted at 10-06 and was never a shipped tag. The ?for=keys link and three dead /c/<id>/ addresses are recorded in REQUIREMENTS.md CAT-01 as decided costs. PREV_FILES 84 + 0 = 84, PREV_TESTS 877 - 3 = 874 - the phase's first NEGATIVE term, and check-counts.mjs treats it exactly like a positive one because it is a plain equality in both columns. Sweep 4 19 in 136 s, e2e 87 + 0 titles and 106 + 0 runs, check 579 / 0 / 0, static/og 26 images / 155,278 B.
- [Phase 12]: 12-05: 550 versus 556 for NINE PADS at 4x4 is settled by measurement and NEITHER figure is wrong - 550 is cost(compile(state)) on the SHIPPED card, which still carries preset:"ninepads" and its twelve-character #z.pninepads marker; 556 is what a TUNED state lands, because withChange deletes preset and the marker becomes an eighteen-character field dump. Shipped 580/550, tuned 586/556, +6 in both directions. presets.ts carries 550, because that is what presets.spec.ts test 4 re-measures
- [Phase 12]: 12-05: ARC takes the +18 gate (if s.s>0 then F(s.f)end inside the rate branch) and not the +25 early return, because the gate keeps s.r, s.f and s.d tracking while the card is stopped so the resume is exact. It is also NOT a job for 12-07's touch-library hysteresis: that suppresses a MOVE inside one CELL, and cell 40 alone spans r 14 to 18 - five of the thirty-one rate steps - so an in-cell wobble IS a real rate change
- [Phase 12]: 12-05: CONSOLE's muted faders become interactive and SILENT, reversing 11-07's inert reading on the record. All three shipped shapes are written into the entry header and lua-smoke test 8 is rewritten rather than deleted. +8, 844 -> 852 at the RGB444 picker corner, 56 free - it fits, and 56 is the number 12-09 must fit Q into
- [Phase 12]: 12-05: the widget rule is widened KIND-BLIND - a knob of any kind whose values are at most two integers renders as words, INTEGER_WORD_ROW_MAX 2. Shape (b) of the two costed; (a), admitting count to WORD_KINDS, was refused because it needs a wordFor('count') branch that duplicates integerReadout and leaves a two-valued size or amount knob a rail for no stateable reason. It reads its values because STARFIELD's edge is feel with 'soft' and 'hard', and an n-only rule would render it as a word row labelled '1 of 2'
- [Phase 12]: 12-05: the reachability sweep's two-pass separability licence is FALSE on NINE PADS - at 3x3 the compiler emits the chosen colour AND a dimmed variant, at 4x4 once - so moving the shipped grid moved which literal Pass B calls dearest and the pinned margin reports 268 -> 271. The TRUE worst is still 640 of 908, unmoved, and colour-picker.spec.ts pins it independently. Recorded in the sweep's own comment, in REQUIREMENTS.md's TUNE-05 row and in the phase's deferred-items.md
- [Phase 12]: **The touch library is 769 of 908 canonical, 139 free, and no fallback fired.** Measured under the pinned compressScript after padReady() and a fixed point of itself; the minifier's one edit is the space between P={} and function W, so TOUCH_LIBRARY is built with that join and library.spec.ts asserts the uniform 770-character join compresses to exactly the shipped string. Neither A nor D was dropped
- [Phase 12]: **Q expires contact i on EVERY onset, unconditionally, and R's idempotence is the contract instead of a nine-character guard.** With 139 free the question was never budget - it is which contract is cheaper to keep true. R therefore fires on a contact's first press too, measured in wasmoon, and library.ts section 3 says so where CHORUS will read it in 12-09
- [Phase 12]: **F stays dropped and its 123 characters stay returned, with O and L.** Nothing in this phase calls it; every card gives the finger's feedback itself. It comes back only with a caller named
- [Phase 12]: **A preset still lands the EMPTY page init, not SYSTEM_DEFAULT_SETUP.** ladder.spec.ts:275 refuses lib/protocol to every file under src/lib/tune/, so a firmware default cannot be named on the tuner's side of that line; install.svelte.ts's #pageInit is the one place the substitution happens and 12-03's install.spec.ts proves it. The plan's 'a preset's equals SYSTEM_DEFAULT_SETUP' cannot be asserted where it asked
- [Phase 12]: **The library's gate is a server spec by choice and the sweep's member list stays 4 19.** The sweep exists for the load-sensitive measurement; this is one string measured once, and it should be checked per run rather than per wave
- [Phase 12]: 12-08: SONAR re-fitted with the three the bench named, because it carried the same guard byte for byte - and all four toggle, so the plan's reason for singling it out does not exist
- [Phase 12]: 12-08: All four sequencers take X(s,20) in the Timer they already had, reversing the plan's own earlier reasoning - Q's onset rules need a press and a contact whose lift was lost may never press again
- [Phase 12]: 12-08: QUADRANT deferred, not re-fitted, even though R-replaces-the-branch plus a Timer FITS at 874 of 908 - it needs a release-path refactor on the one entry that handles code 9 explicitly, a period nobody has chosen, and a test the declared term has no room for. Owner 12-12
- [Phase 12]: 12-08: touch-guard.spec.ts gains a delegation arm - a body with no event-code chain is REQUIRED to call Q, bounded from both ends rather than against a count of delegating entries
- [Phase 12]: 12-09: CHORUS defines the library's R convention and releases through R(s,s.c) rather than an inline note-off loop, so the release is written once and reached from all four expiry paths; its private per-contact watchdog becomes X(self,20) at the same two-second window
- [Phase 12]: 12-09: CONSOLE ships WITHOUT self.q - 781 against 847 at the picker corner, and both shapes pass the mute-row swipe and resting-finger probes, so the 66 characters buy nothing
- [Phase 12]: 12-09: MORPH does NOT return on Q's nil - the research's call shape would freeze the bilinear weights inside a cell, measured at 4 messages against 16, so MORPH keeps its own end test and calls Q for the trail cell alone

- [Phase 12]: 12-11: LUMEN's two axis CCs go through the library's A - the probe's rule 3 gets its only call site - and @CC+1 now carries 127-y, which is a WIRE CHANGE taken from the user's own bench snippet and named in the entry header and audition row 15

### Blockers/Concerns

- [Phase 2] RESOLVED 2026-09-03 by the hardware run (02-04): a bare browser page completed the whole cycle against a real ZONA RevH on firmware 1.5.5, active page 3, ~22:15-22:23 UTC, with zero timeouts, zero NACKs and zero refused frames; and the host heartbeat is NOT required - arm B ran it off throughout and every write and store was still acknowledged. What remains untested against hardware is every FAILURE path: no unplug mid-write, no port conflict (checklist step 0 was not exercised), no timeout and no negative acknowledgement has ever been seen from a real module.
- [Phase 5] RESOLVED 2026-09-04 by the entry-consistency check (05-05, `stamp.ts` `decodeFor`): a stamp whose preset does not match the route's entry lands `unreadable` on the base configuration; `#z.pdial` under aurora is refused, `#z.paurora` restores. The base36 stamp checksum hole in prior art (a relabelled stamp decoding to a different card) is closed for HANGAR's routes.
- [Phase 7] Flash unplug-during-store ordering: firmware writes Setup before Timer to flash, opposite of the RAM write order; no software mitigation exists or is planned (the store is one broadcast and the firmware decides the order). Carried by 07-13 as docs/INSTALL-RUNBOOK.md row E's caution ("do not pull the cable while the label reads KEEPING… or PUTTING BACK…") and deferred item 26; stays open until row E puts the store's flash-write duration on the record.

Phase 7's success criteria 1 to 5 are verified-by-user-pending in their (hardware) halves: whether a real ZONA on 1.5.5 answers SERIALNUMBER/FETCH (row A - the key every durable claim hangs off; source-verified, never observed), the first RAM write and its wall time and whether 0 ms pacing survived two full-size writes (row B), PUT BACK by eye (row C), a power cycle bringing the original back (row D), a store surviving a power cycle and its re-fetch round count (row E), a fresh-tab PUT BACK from the browser's own storage (row F), and an optional rig (row G). Every install state is proven only against a scripted serial and a scripted ZONA modelled on a source reading of the firmware and on Phase 2's frames. Phase 6's six rows are still unanswered too, and the install runbook exercises the connect and the replug in passing.

Rule 1: a single signed index step cannot torus-wrap a 9x9 grid, because the row step is step//9 and Lua floor-divides -1 to MINUS ONE, so a left-steered snake would have climbed a row every step — two signed fields and (h//9+v)%9*9+(h%9+u)%9 is the whole move; LIFE's living branch needs glpfs(a,2,255,0,0) and not glp, because glp leaves the rate and a cell returning from the dying layer would keep 247 and fade straight back out. Rule 2: the plan's SNAKE never dies — nothing turns it, so it circles one row forever and the plan's own behaviour clause could not hold; a perpendicular-only greedy autopilot fixed it and the self-played game is five bites and a death, repeated exactly, every 6.5 seconds.
